uses X=v+e+z_loc<=(2+tau)max(v,z_loc). Since d<tau X,

    d^2*e^2*D/(u^2*v^2*w)
       <2tau^4(2+tau)^2<25/32<1.

This is monotonicity of this explicit scalar ratio only, not a
monotonicity assertion about the endpoint optimization problem.

Still with l>=4, suppose z_loc<13/4. Then e<65/48<3/2,
v>X-5, e^2<3, and e^2/w<2 by (4.3). Moreover
d+v+e<(1+tau)X<3X/2. Therefore

    d^2*e^2*D/(u^2*v^2*w)
      <tau^2*X^2/(u^2*v^2)*(3+2u+3X)
      <=tau^2*(3+5X)/v^2
      <(3+5X)/(4(X-5)^2)<1.

For the final inequality, writing X=16+x gives
4(X-5)^2-(3+5X)=4x^2+83x+401>0.

Finally l<=3 implies X<c^3<(5/2)^3<16 and d,e<7, while
the ACTUAL D=X_m-epsilon>2^20. If u>=D/4, then v>=1,w>9/10 give

    d^2*e^2*D/(u^2*v^2*w)<384160/(9D)<1.

If u<D/4, the actual outer mass is
O=D-u-d-X+epsilon>3D/4-23>D/2, and w>O-13/8>D/3.
Using d/u<tau and e/v<tau gives the ratio <3tau^4<3/16<1.
All cases are exhausted. Hence

                         Delta>0.                  (5.1)

## 6. Exact ellipse, without deleting the multiplier constraints

Set

    sigma0=hc/Kc, alpha_max=A/Ec,
    a0=(u*v+2D*d)/(2D*Lc), b0=d*hc/(2w*Lc),
    alpha_opt(sigma)=a0-b0*sigma,
    R(sigma)=(Kc*sigma-hc)/Lc,
    eta=(A-Ec*alpha)/Lc,
    beta=1-sigma-alpha, gamma=sigma, ell=alpha-R(sigma).

These satisfy both contact equations (3.2) identically. All original
multiplier signs are equivalent to

    sigma>=sigma0,
    R(sigma)<=alpha<=min(alpha_max,1-sigma).         (6.1)

Here sigma>=0 and sigma<=1 follow from (6.1). In particular this
is exactly the multiplier polygon, not an unspecified feasible matrix.
Equation (4.4) implies

    0<sigma0<sqrt(w/D)<1,
    w-D*sigma0^2
      =(U+e)[U*w-e*(e+w)]/Kc^2>0.                  (6.2)

Let y=2D*alpha-A-d*Kc/Lc and

    M2=u^2*v^2+4D*d^2*(e+w).

Using A*Lc-d*U=u*v in (3.3) gives

    J11=M2/(4D*Lc^2)-y^2/(4D),
    J22=w-D*sigma^2,
    J12=(sigma*y+d*hc/Lc)/2.                       (6.3)

For example substituting eta in J11 yields
-D*alpha^2+(u*v+2D*d)*alpha/Lc-d*A/Lc;
completing this square proves its displayed constant. Completing
the determinant square, and using
4w(e+w)-hc^2=-e^2, gives exactly

    det J=(w-D*sigma^2)*Delta/(4D*w*Lc^2)
                      -D*w*(alpha-alpha_opt(sigma))^2.          (6.4)

In particular det J>=0 is an ellipse. Positivity of Delta alone
does not establish that it meets (6.1).

The center of its horizontal section at sigma0 is

    alpha_c=alpha_opt(sigma0)
      =[u*v/(2D)+d*(2w*U-e^2)/(2w*Kc)]/Lc.          (6.5)

It satisfies alpha_c>0 and alpha_c<1-sigma0. For positivity use
2wU>e^2 from (4.4). For the second inequality multiply by
2D*w*Kc*Lc: the difference has numerator

    v*w*(2D*U-u*Kc)+2D*w*Lc*e+D*d*e^2>0,

because 2DU-uKc=2D(U-u)+uU>0. Thus this point satisfies EVERY
multiplier half-plane except possibly alpha<=alpha_max.

If alpha_c>alpha_max, feasibility can be tested entirely on the
eta=0 edge alpha=alpha_max. Indeed (alpha_c,sigma0) lies strictly
inside the ellipse and satisfies all the other half-planes. For any
feasible point, the line segment to this center stays in the ellipse
and those other half-planes and meets alpha=alpha_max. This also
explains why an unconstrained-center argument alone was incomplete.
The explicit proof below supplies a point on that edge.

## 7. Entire eta=0 section and its true upper endpoint

Put alpha=alpha_max, eta=0, j_cap as in (4.6), and define

    Hc=d*Kc-alpha_max*u*Lc,
    sigma_star=d*hc*Hc/M2,
    sigma_A=(hc+Lc*alpha_max)/Kc.

On this edge, beta and ell can equivalently be parameterized by

    beta=(B+ell*Lc)/Kc,
    sigma=1-alpha_max-beta.

Hence sigma ranges over exactly [sigma0,sigma_A] as ell decreases
from alpha_max to0. Notice the STRICT identity

    1-alpha_max-sigma_A=B/Kc>0.                    (7.1)

Thus beta>=0 imposes no extra restriction on this edge. The full
determinant reduces to the literal concave quadratic

    det J=j_cap*(w-D*sigma^2)
                       -(d*hc-Hc*sigma)^2/(4Lc^2)
         =j_cap*Delta/M2
                       -M2*(sigma-sigma_star)^2/(4Lc^2).        (7.2)

To check the constant and curvature, use
M2=Hc^2+4D*Lc^2*j_cap and w*M2-D*d^2*hc^2=Delta.
In particular its unconstrained maximum is strictly positive.

The following sign calculation removes the upper clamping penalty
whenever alpha_c>alpha_max. In that case Hc>0, since
Hc=2D*Lc*(a0-alpha_max) and a0>alpha_c. We claim

                  v*Ec*Kc-2D*B*Lc>0.              (7.3)

Write Y=e+w>0 and q_ratio=e/v. We have d<5u/12, v<u,
Lc<17u/12, q_ratio<5/12, and by (1.1),(1.2),

    e< (5/17)*(u-v).

Expanding after division of (7.3) by v gives

    Ec*Kc-2D*Lc*(1+q_ratio)
      =u^2+(1-2q_ratio)*u*Lc-2q_ratio*Lc^2
        +4uY+(4-2q_ratio)*Lc*Y+4Y^2.               (7.4)

If Lc<=6u/5, replace q_ratio by5/12 to lower-bound the first
three terms by (u+Lc)*(u-5Lc/6)>=0; the Y terms are positive.
If Lc>6u/5, then v>47u/60 and
q_ratio<(5/17)*(13/47)=65/799<1/12. Replacing q_ratio by1/12
instead bounds those terms below by (u+Lc)*(u-Lc/6)>0 because
Lc<17u/12<6u. Again the Y terms are positive. This proves (7.3)
at every depth with the ACTUAL u and w; no parameter was reset.

For completeness, derive the needed derivative using increments
p-r and q-r, an invertible change from (p-q,q-r) with determinant1.
With alpha=alpha_max and eta=0 the entries in these coordinates are

    j_cap,
    (beta+ell)*v-D*beta^2,
    (ell*d-beta*alpha_max*u)/2.

At ell=0, beta=B/Kc. The derivative of their determinant with
respect to ell is exactly

    [j_cap*(v*Ec*Kc-2D*B*Lc)
                 +B*alpha_max*u*Hc/2]/Kc^2>0.       (7.5)

Since d sigma/d ell=-Lc/Kc<0, (7.5) says that the derivative
in sigma at sigma_A is negative. The strictly concave quadratic
(7.2) therefore has

                 0<sigma_star<sigma_A.             (7.6)

Moreover sigma_star<sqrt(w/D): (7.2) has positive value there,
and j_cap>0 in the first expression of (7.2) forces
w-D*sigma_star^2>0. This proves that only the lower clamp at
sigma0 can still matter.

## 8. The lower clamping cost is paid explicitly

The diagonal-cycle case u*v>=d*U will be handled separately in
Section9. Assume here that

    u*v<d*U, alpha_c>alpha_max.                     (8.1)

If sigma_star>=sigma0, the positive maximum in (7.2) is admissible
by (7.6). Suppose instead that sigma_star<sigma0. We show that
the lower-clamped choice sigma=sigma0 still has positive determinant.

The left root of det J=0 on the horizontal line sigma=sigma0 is

    alpha_left=alpha_c
             -sqrt((w-D*sigma0^2)*Delta)/(2D*w*Lc).

Define the explicit tangency threshold

    alpha_tan=a0-M2/(2D*Lc*d*Kc).

Because Hc=2D*Lc*(a0-alpha_max), the inequality
sigma_star<sigma0 is equivalent to alpha_max>alpha_tan.
On the other hand, direct subtraction gives

    alpha_left-alpha_tan
      =sqrt(Delta)/(2D*w*Lc*Kc)
          *[sqrt(Delta)/d-sqrt(w*Kc^2-D*hc^2)].       (8.2)

All square roots are real and strictly positive by (5.1),(6.2).
The exact identities

    w*Kc^2-D*hc^2=w*U^2-D*e^2,
    Delta/d^2=w*(u*v/d)^2-D*e^2

and u*v<d*U prove that the bracket in (8.2) is strictly negative.
Consequently

    alpha_left<alpha_tan<alpha_max<alpha_c.

The determinant on that horizontal line is the concave quadratic
in (6.4), positive strictly between its two roots. Its right root
is larger than alpha_c. Thus det J(alpha_max,sigma0)>0.

This is the previously missing clamping estimate. It does not infer
the clamped value from Delta>0: (8.2) compares the actual boundary
penalty against the ellipse radius using the physical case distinction.

## 9. Explicit uniform coefficient rule and full PSD

Here is a literal choice for every ancestor pair and each charge.
All quantities are closed expressions in the original physical masses.

CASE I: u*v>=d*U.

    alpha=A/(2D), eta=alpha*u/Lc,
    theta=C/Kc, sigma=1-theta=sigma0,
    beta=theta-alpha, gamma=sigma, R=0, ell=alpha.

Both contact equations hold, because eta*Lc=alpha*u and
2D*alpha=A. The signs are explicit: alpha,eta,ell>0,
0<theta<1, and theta>alpha because C>A and Kc<2D.
The entire increment matrix is diagonal:

    J12=0,
    J11=alpha*(u*v-d*U)/(2Lc)>=0,
    J22=(U+e)[U*w-e*(e+w)]/Kc^2>0.                 (9.1)

This includes equality u*v=d*U. The mixed coefficient is exactly0,
so (9.1) proves full PSD, including that boundary case.

CASE II: u*v<d*U and alpha_c<=alpha_max.

    sigma=sigma0, alpha=alpha_c,
    R=0, eta=(A-Ec*alpha)/Lc,
    beta=1-sigma-alpha, gamma=sigma, ell=alpha.

Section6 proves alpha>0,beta>0,gamma>0; the assumed comparison
gives eta>=0. All row signs and both contact equations therefore
hold. Equations (5.1),(6.2),(6.4) give

    J22>0,
    det J=(w-D*sigma0^2)*Delta/(4D*w*Lc^2)>0.        (9.2)

CASE III: u*v<d*U and alpha_c>alpha_max.

    alpha=alpha_max, eta=0,
    sigma=max(sigma0,sigma_star),
    R=(Kc*sigma-hc)/Lc,
    beta=1-sigma-alpha, gamma=sigma, ell=alpha-R.

Sections7--8 prove sigma0<=sigma<sigma_A and
sigma<sqrt(w/D). Thus 0<=R<alpha, ell>0, beta>B/Kc>0,
and gamma>0. Every final multiplier in Section3 is nonnegative.
Both contact equations hold by their defining formulas. If
sigma=sigma_star, (7.2) gives det J=j_cap*Delta/M2>0.
If sigma=sigma0>sigma_star, Section8 gives det J>0.
Also J22=w-D*sigma^2>0 in either subcase.

For Cases II and III, the ENTIRE increment quadratic has the explicit
square representation

    J22*||tvec+(J12/J22)*s||^2+(det J/J22)*||s||^2.   (9.3)

Together with (9.1) in Case I and all squares in (3.4), this proves
full PSD on every real equal-sector vector assignment in every case.
There is no numerical pair list, unknown matrix, optimization oracle,
or assumption of favorable angles.

Finally these choices solve precisely the former scalar interface
(10.2)--(10.5) of a79d801952174a0a. They have

    sigma0<=sigma<sqrt(w/D),
    sigma<=1,
    sigma<=(hc+Lc*alpha_max)/Kc,
    sigma<=(hc+Lc)/(Kc+Lc),

because their alpha obeys (6.1). At this sigma our explicit alpha
has det J>=0. The nearest-point alpha_clamp of alpha_opt to the
same permitted alpha interval can only increase the determinant in
(6.4). Therefore the literal clamped inequality (10.5) holds too.
In Cases II and III the determinant is strictly positive; Case I
may be semidefinite exactly at u*v=d*U.

## 10. A failed tempting choice, on the SAME physical hierarchy

This diagnosis is included to distinguish the proof above from the
invalid shortcut of ignoring the alpha upper bound. For every
m>=1536 choose

    j=2^(m-2)-1, k=j+1,
    X=X_(m-2), u=X, v=1, d=a*c^(m-2), e=a,
    D=4X+(2+c)d-epsilon.

The actual suffix is T=3X+(1+c)d-2-a, so this is a genuine ancestor
pair, with its full exterior mass. It has X>=2048,
5X<D<6X, d>2X/5, d<X/2, U>X, Lc>d, w>3X.

The sign of alpha_c-alpha_max, after multiplication by the positive
denominator 2D*Kc*Ec*Lc, is the sign of

    N=2D*d*U*Lc-u^2*v*Kc-D*d*e^2*Ec/w.

For the displayed pair the positive term is greater than
(8/5)X^4. The next term is smaller than12X^3, and the last is
smaller than3X^2, using e<1/2, Ec<12X, and w>3X. Thus

    N>(8/5)X^4-12X^3-3X^2>0

for X>=2048; the last sign follows already from X>=16 by dividing
by X^2 and observing (8/5)X^2-12X-3 is increasing there and positive
at16. Hence the unconstrained center at sigma0 has eta<0 at every
one of these actual pairs. It is NOT a legal certificate. The rule
in Section9 repairs this failure by the explicit eta=0 boundary.
This is a counterexample to that coefficient choice only, not a
primal escape or a failure of the endpoint upper bound.

## 11. Objective consequence and exact scope boundary

Applying (3.4),(9.1)--(9.3) to any legal static transcript proves
the upper inequalities (1.4). This works after translation to ANY
minimizer of that very function, and therefore also its own closest
minimizer. There is no substituted radius or inherited minimum set.

The matching member for E is the single scalar Huber function

    kappa=1/Z,
    f_H(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=kappa,
    f_H(x)=kappa*|x|-kappa^2/2 otherwise, x_0=1.

Its derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and1-Lipschitz, and its
complete minimum set is {0}. At every actual cumulative mass s<=S_m,
x=1-kappa*s>kappa, so the gradient is kappa. Every original step
is executed, with p=q=r=g_n=kappa and own radius1. Direct substitution
gives 2f_H(x_n)=2/Z-Z/Z^2=1/Z. This also belongs to Q.

The original benchmark B_n is the all-integer-split benchmark in
accepted931b9653ce0f7201, with U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Its unconditional
comparison B_n/Z<47/50 applies to precisely this unchanged H_m.
Since d0^2<9/4 and Z>=2^(m+1)-1>1000*(9/4), (1.4) gives

    B_n sup_E V<47/50,
    B_n sup_Q V<940/999<19/20.

These are ancestor-pair, three-constant-sector statements only.
Even combining this joint draft with the previous outside-subtree
draft would still concern three constant queried-gradient sectors.
The function-class supremum W has independent possible gradients
inside each block. Neither this report nor the unverified sparse
simplex finding a753ad89e79ede74 supplies a lossless restriction to
three or four sector values. The full-class hypothesis
W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) in accepted931b9653ce0f7201 is still absent.
The original unrestricted asymptotic minimax problem remains OPEN.

## 12. Second mechanism: fully paid qr cycle, retained but unnecessary

This fallback was explored before Sections7--8 closed the first
mechanism. It is not needed as a premise for Sections1--11.
Let R_1=n for E and R_1=n-1 for Q, the last available r endpoint.
Its actual sector mass is t_r=T or T-d0. Put z_q=t_r+1.
Adding BOTH original rows Q_(L_q,R_1), Q_(R_1,L_q) with multiplier
xi>=0 adds exactly

    xi*C_qr=xi*[-e||q||^2+(e-z_q)<q,r>+z_q||r||^2]. (12.1)

The term -xi*e||q||^2 is a real cost. In particular

    z_q=w+1/2 for E,
    z_q=w+a^2/2 for Q.                             (12.2)

For Q one may NOT use the terminal equality g_n=r to replace this
endpoint. The same original terminal rows and square (2.3) remain.
Core value flow is unchanged by (12.1). The full quadratic (3.1)
acquires -xi*e on its q-square, xi*z_q on its r-square, and
xi*(e-z_q) on its qr coefficient. Its PAID contacts are

    alpha*Ec=A-eta*Lc,
    theta*Kc=C-R*Lc-xi*(e+z_q).                    (12.3)

Thus R=[Kc*sigma-hc-xi*(e+z_q)]/Lc. The complete increment entries
are still J11 in (3.3), and

    J22=w+xi*z_q-D*sigma^2,
    J12=[sigma*(2D*alpha-A)-R*d]/2.                 (12.4)

The full identity (3.4) holds with these entries and the additional
literal original row weights xi, all ultimately doubled to lambda.

At fixed alpha,sigma, the exact sign interval for xi is

    xi_low=max(0,(Kc*sigma-hc-Lc*alpha)/(e+z_q)),
    xi_high=(Kc*sigma-hc)/(e+z_q),                  (12.5)

provided sigma>=sigma0, 0<=alpha<=alpha_max, and alpha+sigma<=1.
These conditions are equivalent to all signs: (12.5) enforces
0<=R<=alpha, the first contact gives eta>=0, and beta,gamma>=0
are explicit. All other table entries are sums of these weights.

For further exact elimination set J12_0 to the xi=0 expression,
j11=J11, and b_xi=d*(e+z_q)/(2Lc)>0. Then

    det J=det J_at_0
             +xi*(z_q*j11-2b_xi*J12_0)-b_xi^2*xi^2.             (12.6)

For j11>0, the candidate maximizing this determinant on its entire
sign interval is the explicit clipping of

    xi_stat=(z_q*j11-2b_xi*J12_0)/(2b_xi^2)

to [xi_low,xi_high]. This follows by completing the square in the
strictly concave quadratic (12.6), not by invoking an optimizer.
Nonnegative determinant then implies the entire J is PSD because
j11>0; no diagonal-only check is used.

There is also a useful audit of the paid cost. Define

    w_xi=w+xi*z_q, h_xi=hc+xi*(e+z_q),
    Delta_xi=w_xi*M2-D*d^2*h_xi^2,
    alpha_opt_xi=a0-d*h_xi*sigma/(2w_xi*Lc).

The same square completion as Section6 gives

    det J=(w_xi-D*sigma^2)*Delta_xi/(4D*w_xi*Lc^2)
                      -D*w_xi*(alpha-alpha_opt_xi)^2,

    Delta_xi=Delta
       +xi*[z_q*u^2*v^2+2D*d^2*e*(z_q-e-2w)]
       -D*d^2*(e+z_q)^2*xi^2.                      (12.7)

The strictly negative xi^2 term shows why simply increasing the
backward cycle is not a monotone repair. At sigma=sigma0, (12.5)
even forces xi=0, and the first contact in (12.3) is independent
of xi. Thus adding this cycle while freezing the illegal center
from Section10 cannot repair its negative eta. No failure of the
full endpoint cone or legal primal counterexample follows from that
observation. The first mechanism already supplies a legal xi=0 choice.

## 13. Handoff, verification status, and work accounting

The concrete next correctness check is the joint draft in Sections1--11,
especially the upper-edge derivative sign (7.3)--(7.6) and the
lower-clamp comparison (8.2). Those two steps close the previous
inequality (10.5). All quantities, physical hypotheses, original
multipliers, and the full PSD square are printed in this report.

No verifier has accepted this proof. Shared publication is awareness
only. No new result may be treated as a fact-graph premise. There
was no fact_submit allowance, no new fact_id, and no finalization,
revocation, shutdown, outward publication, or Goal-completion action.
The invocation returns at cap1, without renewal or TASK modification.

The invoked skills were query-memory, direct-proving, and
construct-counterexamples. The one Matlas query returned two unrelated
statements (arXiv:2102.07922 Lemma6 and arXiv:2105.12062 Lemma1);
neither was used. A background web query located Taylor--Hendrickx--
Glineur, Smooth Strongly Convex Interpolation and Exact Worst-case
Performance of First-order Methods, arXiv:1502.05666,
https://arxiv.org/abs/1502.05666. The existing own extracted text was
read around its interpolation construction; it supplies no proof
premise beyond accepted1252b8823bb5a290 here. Broad initial recall
output was truncated, so the needed prior-report sections and accepted
scope passages were reread separately. No truncated passage was used
as a mathematical premise.

# A two-dimensional exact counterexample to the asymmetric eight-step seed

Status: complete UNVERIFIED counterexample, worker max, 2026-09-26.
No verifier or numerical solver call. This attacks the precise schedule
in finding 29a1f4a5269032b1, not a generic three-block rule and not the
unrestricted minimax target. Every needed calculation is included below.

## 1. Exact candidate and elementary parameter bounds

Define

    u=10/7, C=1+2u=27/7,
    D=(165+sqrt(65641))/42,
    s=D-55/7,
    T=(4+D+sqrt((4+D)^2+16D))/2,
    r=T-4-D.

The actual prescribed schedule is

    h=(3/2,3/2,1+r,3/2,3/2,1+s,10/7,10/7).           (1)

Its physical capacity is T=1+sum h_i=83/7+r+s. Directly from the
two quadratic equations,

    D s=196/9, rT=4D, Trs=784/9.                       (2)

For later exact estimates,

    10<D<21/2, 12/5<r<5/2, 0<s<98/45.                 (3)

Indeed P(D)=D^2-(55/7)D-196/9 is negative at10, with P(10)=-22/63,
and positive at21/2, with P(21/2)=215/36. Its positive root lies
between those values. The positive root r of
r^2+(4+D)r-4D=0 exceeds12/5 because the polynomial there is
384/25-(8/5)D<0. It is less than5/2 because its value there is
65/4-(3/2)D>1/2. The s bounds follow from (2) and D>10.

Put

    kappa=s/C=7s/27, so 0<kappa<686/1215<3/5,
    v=sqrt((5r-8)/800)>0,
    g_A=(1/4,0),
    g_B=(-1/10,v),
    g_C=-kappa g_B.                                    (4)

In particular

    ||g_B||^2=r/160,
    <g_A,g_B>=-1/40,
    <g_A,g_C>=kappa/40.                                (5)

## 2. One globally fixed function and the actual GD trajectory

On R^2 define, before running GD,

    J(z)=max(0,<g_A,z>,<g_B,z>,<g_C,z>),
    f(x)=min_z[J(z)+||x-z||^2/2].                       (6)

This is one finite convex polyhedral function and its unit Moreau
envelope. The quadratic proximal objective is coercive and strictly
convex, so its minimizer is unique. Subgradient monotonicity gives
||grad f(x)-grad f(y)||^2<=<grad f(x)-grad f(y),x-y>;
the minimizing inequalities identify grad f=x-prox_J(x). Thus f is
globally convex, differentiable, and 1-smooth. It is nonnegative and
minimized at origin. The two gradient directions g_A and g_B are not
collinear because v>0.

Start at x_0=(1,0)=4g_A. The displayed queries and gradients are

    x_0=(1,0),                         grad f(x_0)=g_A,
    x_1=(5/8,0),                       grad f(x_1)=g_A,
    x_2=(1/4,0),                       grad f(x_2)=g_A,
    x_3=(-r/4,0),                      grad f(x_3)=g_B,
    x_4=x_3-(3/2)g_B,                  grad f(x_4)=g_B,
    x_5=x_3-3g_B,                      grad f(x_5)=g_B,
    x_6=x_3-(4+s)g_B,                  grad f(x_6)=g_C,
    x_7=x_6-u g_C,                     grad f(x_7)=g_C,
    x_8=x_6-2u g_C,                    grad f(x_8)=g_C.    (7)

Define the residual point

    Z=x_3-4g_B=(-r/4+2/5,-4v).

From (5), <g_B,Z>=r/40-4r/160=0, hence also <g_C,Z>=0.
Since Ckappa=s and C=1+2u,

    x_6=Z+Cg_C, x_8=Z+g_C.                             (8)

The arithmetic in (7) proves the GD updates for (1); the next paragraph
checks that all these gradients are the actual gradients of (6).

### Every old and future affine plane, on all three blocks

If g is an active plane normal in J at x-g, its subgradient inequality
proves that x-g uniquely minimizes the proximal objective. Therefore
it suffices to compare all four planes there.

On the first pair and its following checkpoint query, write the query
as (4-t)g_A, with ordinary cumulative t in [0,3]. Its proposed
proximal point is d g_A, d=3-t>=0. The current plane has value d/16.
The B plane has value -d/40<=0. The C plane has value d kappa/40,
whose ratio to the current value when d>0 is 2kappa/5<1. The zero
plane is also dominated, including the case d=0. This checks both
future planes at ALL first-block queries.

On the middle pair and its following checkpoint query, write the query
as x_3-tg_B, t in [0,3]. Its proposed proximal point is Z+d g_B,
d=3-t>=0. The current B value is d r/160. The future C value is
-kappa d r/160<=0. The old A value is

    <g_A,Z>+d<g_A,g_B>=-(5r-8)/80-d/40<0.

Thus every plane, including zero, is dominated there too.

On the last pair and at the final point, the proposed proximal point is
Z+d g_C with 0<=d<=C-1=20/7. The selected C value is
d kappa^2 r/160, the B value is its negative multiple -1/kappa,
and zero is dominated. The old A comparison is exactly

    2(5r-8)>=d kappa(4-r kappa).                        (9)

Its left side is greater than8 by (3). If 4-r kappa were negative
the right side would be nonpositive. Otherwise, using (3)--(4), it is
at most

    (20/7)*(3/5)*4=48/7<8.

Hence (9) holds strictly. This checks the potentially harmful positive
correlation with the remote first-block plane. It is the constraint
omitted by a purely scalar block gluing or a quadratic-product test.

All gradients in (7) are therefore actual gradients of the same fixed
function (6). The zero set is nonempty and the true minimum is zero.
Moreover origin is closest to x_0: any minimizing z satisfies
<g_A,z><=0, hence its first coordinate is nonpositive and
||(1,0)-z||^2>=1. The actual initial distance is exactly one.

## 3. Exact negative primitive energy

The proposed saturated primitive inequality, with physical capacity
T=1+sum_(i=0)^7 h_i and S=T-1, requires nonnegativity of

    V_h=2 sum_(i=0)^7 h_i(f_i-f_8)
       +sum_(i=0)^7 h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
       -S T ||g_8||^2,                                (10)

for every convex 1-smooth GD trajectory. This is the precise primitive
energy being tested; no nonsaturated objective certificate is rejected
by merely giving it this name.

Every sampled gradient here is one of the active homogeneous plane
normals, so its actual value satisfies

    f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2, including i=8.             (11)

In particular every optimum interpolation residual is zero. Substituting
(11) into (10), and using x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, gives the complete telescope

    2sum h_i f_i+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
      =sum[2h_i<g_i,x_i>-h_i^2||g_i||^2]
      =||x_0||^2-||x_8||^2,

    V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_8+Sg_8||^2.                      (12)

By (8), g_8=g_C, x_8=Z+g_C and <Z,g_C>=0. A direct calculation gives

    ||Z||^2=r(5r-8)/80,
    T^2||g_C||^2=T^2 r s^2/(160 C^2)
                 =941192/(295245 r),                  (13)

where (2) was used in the second equality. Hence the EXACT energy is

    V_h=1-r(5r-8)/80-941192/(295245 r).                  (14)

This is strictly negative with a rational margin. Since r<5/2,

    T^2||g_C||^2 >1882384/1476225>1,
    V_h < -406159/1476225 <0.                           (15)

The additional term ||Z||^2 is positive and strengthens the rejection.
No numerical residual, optimizer output, or limiting argument is needed.

## 4. Scope

Equations (6)--(15) give a legal two-dimensional counterexample to the
exact eight-step schedule (1) being primitive at its proposed physical
capacity T. It therefore cannot be used as a primitive seed for the
conditional amplification route in that form. Passing all original
single-cut tests and full-curvature equality does not save that seed.

This does not rule out a weaker objective certificate for the same
schedule, different bridge parameters, or any unrestricted minimax
statement. It is a new unverified shared counterexample; no correctness
claim is being promoted into the fact graph without a verifier allocation.

# Max47: recurrent signed reset directions, and the next original row

Status: complete joint analytical draft, UNVERIFIED. No fact_id; no formal
submission is authorized. This is the final allocated round after the
completed45 baseline. The original all-n/all-nonnegative-word exact-profile
goal remains OPEN. Every new argument used below is reproduced here.
Earlier unverified closures are motivation, not correctness premises.

## 1. What changed, and what the evidence does and does not say

The second and final declared template is T2_recurrent_signed_carrier
(plan4af8cb8007ee35f8). It changes the dependence law: a persistent
carrier direction returns after an intervening excursion, while two
reused branch directions have negative inner product. The scale-indexed
supports can shrink and grow. The dimension grows with the number of
distinct scales, and a coordinate can change at arbitrarily many cells.
This is not a positive min kernel, a chronological fresh-innovation law,
a one-future tail, or a common-gradient augmentation.

The motivation is the ORIGINAL frozen H6 Gram, not a numerical fit or
an assertion about its exact rank. For example, its saved inner products
and squared norms include

    <g15,g23>=0.0006937998381333206,
    <g15,g31>=0.0005003025123186063,
    ||g15||^2=0.0016594229178722502,
    ||g31||^2=0.0009516573920766777;
    <g23,g31>=0.0008700101349075905>0,
    <g31,g39>=0.0005328988769998643>0,
    <g23,g39>=-0.0027718891731691783<0.

These suggest both recurrence of a previous direction and a triangle of
correlations whose signs cannot be generated by multiplying one positive
kernel by scalar signs. In T2, the three corresponding successive roles
are q_minus,p,q_plus: their adjacent inner products are positive and their
remote inner product is negative. Unlike the previous positive min kernel,
T2 retains this sign pattern and the carrier recurrence explicitly.
It does NOT reproduce every saved correlation. In particular no exact
pattern is asserted of the raw data or the accepted H6 witness.

Constant gradients inside H2 and the local maximal-energy policy remain
restrictions. The substantial internal H2 changes in the frozen outputs
are NOT represented by this template. The point of this second test is
to determine whether changed signed dependence alone repairs the previous
failed row and all the other rows. It repairs that row, but fails another.

at2026-09-30T20:43:55.274662+00:00, child wall time
0.11879143099940848 seconds. Script SHA256:
8f87943a26f836a3df07d6721db1f5ed183634f532f2f2256f24d1c0d6ba71e7.

0325f4761017bb8ea28086edd6491962594eb9817284a5b03b86b2c4e203e7e9.
Raw output and structured record are under the project directory
research/frozen_structure_20261001/job_04_max47_internal_changes/.
Finding9a299b9a2f951782 published the observations and uncertainty BEFORE
analytical use. Total4/4 jobs and0.4223282469974947 child-wall seconds;
the earlier failed numpy import remains charged. H5 was recorded optimal,
H6 optimal_inaccurate. Original Gram/value scales were retained. No new
word, trajectory, fit, optimization, all-Q/PSD replay or formal call ran.
All small residuals and signs above remain floating-point observations.

Accepted e9a55f57461f253c is a separate exact FINITE H6 witness, including
its stated gradient-jump obstruction. Its dimension66 is not a radius,
and it supplies no transfer to a changing-enclosing-m child. The only
accepted mathematical inputs used below are1252b8823bb5a290 for necessary
smooth-convex interpolation and b98bb807d514e80d,1fca3e5dd555cb4a,
dfde2fa326c908bf for precisely scoped comparison channels.

## 2. The actual word and an explicit finite-dimensional generating rule

For an integer m>=1000000, put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/m, beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), j<m,
    n=2^m-1, N=2^(m-3).

These SAME enclosing-m parameters are used in every child. The N H3
cells occupy the physical query indices8j,...,8j+7,0<=j<N. Put

    s=sum H2=3+2a+beta,
    A=s+1=4+2a+beta, b=beta*c, D=A+b,
    E=A^2-2Ab-b^2, K=A^2(2A+b), kappa=1/4,
    B_lo=beta*c^2, B_hi=beta*c^3, B_top=beta*c^4.

These B quantities are bridge EXCESSES, unrelated to the benchmark
B_n. The entering physical bridge8j-1 has excess

    B_j=beta*c^(2+nu2(j)),       1<=j<N,                    (1)

where nu2(j) is the exponent of2 dividing j. Its actual step is1+B_j.
Inside each H3 cell the central step is1+b and both H2 halves retain
their three original steps(1+a,1+beta,1+a).

In a two-dimensional orientation plane choose orthonormal e0,e1 and put

    p=e0,
    q_plus=(1/4)e0+(sqrt(15)/4)e1,
    q_minus=(1/4)e0-(sqrt(15)/4)e1.

Let w_j be p for odd j, q_plus for j congruent0 modulo4, and q_minus
for j congruent2 modulo4. Thus

    <p,q_plus>=<p,q_minus>=kappa,
    <q_plus,q_minus>=-7/8,
    ||p||=||q_plus||=||q_minus||=1.                         (2)

Every adjacent pair of orientations has inner product kappa.
The product of the three off-diagonal inner products of
q_minus,p,q_plus is -(7/8)kappa^2<0. A positive kernel multiplied
by scalar signs cannot have this property: the product of the three
sign factors is1. Thus the signed dependence is genuinely different.

For B>0 define, in the regimes used below,

    alpha(B)=b B^2/(K+kappa E B),
    gamma(B)=B(bB-kappa E)/K.                               (3)

Choose t0>0. The EXPLICIT norm law is

    t_j=alpha(B_lo)t_(j-1)             if j is odd,
    t_j=gamma(B_j)t_(j-1)              if j is even.         (4)

Section4 proves that the denominators and regime choices are valid:
odd cells shrink, while every even cell grows. There are no unknown Gram
entries or optimization subproblems in(2)--(4).

For the finite list t0,...,t_(N-1), sort its distinct positive entries as
0<tau1<...<tau_d and put tau0=0. In the Euclidean direct sum of d copies
of the orientation plane, define

    v_j = direct_sum_(ell=1)^d
          [1_(tau_ell<=t_j) sqrt(tau_ell-tau_(ell-1)) w_j]. (5)

The identity

    <v_i,v_j>=min(t_i,t_j)<w_i,w_j>, ||v_j||^2=t_j         (6)

follows by summing the shared coordinates. This is an explicit real
realization in dimension2d, not a PSD feasibility oracle. The shared
coordinates reactivate as t_j increases, and w_j returns repeatedly.
The lowest scale block is present in every reset. Its e1 coordinate has
the successive pattern positive,zero,negative,zero, repeated, so it changes
on every consecutive reset transition. There is no bounded coordinate-use
assumption hidden in this generating rule. Section4 also proves that its
actual span dimension grows unboundedly with m.

## 3. Every physical update, all adjacent rows, and the intended function

For j>=1 set

    u_j=-(B_j v_(j-1)+A v_j)/D.                             (7)

For a concrete first cell set u0=v0. More generally the obstruction below
permits any completion of the earlier history. In cell j use gradient u_j
at8j,...,8j+3 and gradient v_j at8j+4,...,8j+7, with positions

    x_(8j+3)=(1+b)u_j+A v_j,
    x_(8j)=D u_j+A v_j,
    x_(8j+4)=A v_j,
    x_(8j+7)=v_j.                                         (8)

Positions between these endpoints subtract each individual original step
in H2 times its assigned gradient. Since the sum of those steps is s=A-1,
all equations(8) agree. The preceding core reset is v_(j-1), so its GD
step1+B_j gives x_(8j)=-B_j v_(j-1), exactly(7). This checks every physical
update without contracting a cell into a surrogate step.

Initially assign

    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,
    y_i=x_i-g_i,
    x_star=g_star=f_star=0.                               (9)

For the ORIGINAL convention of accepted1252b8823bb5a290,

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<g_i-g_j,y_j>.                                  (10)

No ordered pair is deleted. At the central first-half query of a cell,
with P=||v_(j-1)||^2,T=||v_j||^2,B=B_j, one has

    y=(-bB v_(j-1)+A^2 v_j)/D.

Expanding(10) for source v_j and target u_j gives

    D^2 Q_(8j+4,8j+3)
      =K T+kappa E B min(P,T)-bB^2 P.                    (11)

If T<=P, equality in(11) is T/P=alpha(B). If T>=P, equality is
T/P=gamma(B). This proves the origin of the policy(4), without using
any numerical output as an equality. The coefficient of T is positive
on each relevant branch: K+kappa E B_lo>0 on the odd shrinking branch,
and K>0 on the even growing branch. At an even cell the residual at both
T=0 and T=P is negative, so all smaller T also pass that affine part.
These are therefore the largest norms admitted by this one row.

In fact ALL original adjacent pairs and optimum pairs pass for the data
(7)--(9). The details matter because the eventual obstruction is remote.
Within each constant-gradient half both oriented residuals are zero.
Across the central step the forward residual is zero by(11), while the
reverse residual is

    Q_(8j+3,8j+4)=s(<u_j,v_j>-T)<0,

since <u_j,v_j>=-(B kappa min(P,T)+A T)/D<0.
Across the entering step the forward row into the preceding reset is
Q_(8j,8j-1)=0 because its target normal is zero. The reverse row expands as

 D^2 Q_(8j-1,8j)
  =-B(D-1)(D+B)P
    +A[D+2B-BD] kappa min(P,T)+A^2 T.                   (12)

For the stated m, A>5,b>2,D>7,B>=B_lo>5. Thus D+2B-BD<0.
At an odd cell T<P and A<B, so A^2 T<B^2 P. At an even cell,
using(3),

    A^2 T/P=B(bB+kappa|E|)/(2A+b)<B^2,

because b<5/2,2A+b>12,|E|<20epsilon and B>5. Consequently(12)
is strictly less than -5B^2 P. The first cell has constant gradient v0
throughout and causes no exception.

The outgoing optimum residual Q_(i,star) is zero. The other optimum
residual is Q_(star,i)=-<y_i,g_i>. On a second half y_i is a nonnegative
multiple of v_j. On a first half its smallest scalar product occurs at
the central query; earlier queries add nonnegative multiples of||u_j||^2.
At the central query(11) gives <y,u_j>=<y,v_j>. For an odd cell this is

    (A^2-kappa b B_lo)T/D > 0,                            (13)

since A^2>b B_lo. For an even cell it is

    BP[bB-kappa A^2]/[D(2A+b)] > 0,                      (14)

since bB>=bB_hi>A^2. Formula(14) follows by substituting gamma in
(-bB kappa P+A^2 T)/D and using E+b(2A+b)=A^2.
Thus all the original optimum rows also hold.

There is a legitimate single proposed STATIC function, distinct from the
claim that it generates these data. Let C be the convex hull of0 and all
the supplied gradients and define

    F_C(x)=max_(v in C)(<x,v>-||v||^2/2).                 (15)

Its maximizer is P_C(x), the unique Euclidean projection. Differentiating
squared distance along a segment in C gives
<x-P_C(x),v-P_C(x)><=0 for every v in C. Applying this inequality twice
and adding gives
<x-z,P_C(x)-P_C(z)>>=||P_C(x)-P_C(z)||^2,
and consequently the projection is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two
maximizing expressions gives, for every displacement d,
0<=F_C(x+d)-F_C(x)-<P_C(x),d>
 <=<P_C(x+d)-P_C(x),d><=||d||^2.
This proves differentiability and grad F_C=P_C. Hence F_C
is everywhere finite, convex and1-smooth. It interpolates(9) at a sample
if and only if every normal comparison at that sample holds. The remote
violation below proves it does NOT generate the complete prescribed GD
trajectory. Nor can any other smooth convex function interpolate it.

The minimum and full initial cost of(15) can nevertheless be stated
exactly. F_C>=0, and by testing arbitrarily small positive multiples of
each v in C, its zero set is the polar of cone C. Let V=span{v_j}. From(7),

    -v_(j-1)=(D/B_j)u_j+(A/B_j)v_j,
    -v_j=(D/A)u_j+(B_j/A)v_(j-1).

These are nonnegative conic combinations. For N>=2 they show cone C=V.
Thus argmin F_C=V-perp, with unique minimizer0 if restricted to V.
For u0=v0, x0=(2A+b)v0 lies in V and the TRUE initial squared distance is

    R_C^2=(2A+b)^2 t0.                                  (16)

This is a radius, not the dimension2d. The formal terminal ratio
 t_(N-1)/[(2A+b)^2 t0] is NOT a payoff of F_C, because the complete
prescribed trajectory is not its actual trajectory. No lower bound on
W is inferred from this ratio.

## 4. Finite enclosing-m bounds, with no computation or limiting transfer

We use lambda^2=2lambda+1 and12/5<lambda<5/2. Direct substitution gives

    d0:=lambda^2-A=epsilon+4epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],
    0<d0<(8/3)epsilon,
    (1-epsilon)lambda^2<A<lambda^2,
    (1-epsilon)lambda<b=lambda(1-epsilon^2)<lambda.        (17)

Moreover

    E=(A-lambda b)(A+b/lambda)<0, |E|<20epsilon,
    (1-3epsilon)lambda^7<K<lambda^7.                     (18)

For E<0, d0>=epsilon>lambda^2 epsilon^2. Its absolute value is less
than d0(lambda^2+1)<20epsilon. To bound K below, all factors A,A,2A+b
exceed1-epsilon times their limiting factors, and
(1-epsilon)^3>=1-3epsilon.

For r=2,3,4 write B(r)=beta c^r. Expanding(1-epsilon)(1+epsilon)^r gives

 lambda^r < B(r) <=(1+4epsilon)lambda^r.                 (19)

For r=2 the expansion is1+epsilon-epsilon^2-epsilon^3; for r=3 it is
1+2epsilon-2epsilon^3-epsilon^4; for r=4 it is
1+3epsilon+2epsilon^2-2epsilon^3-3epsilon^4-epsilon^5.
These prove(19) throughout0<epsilon<=10^-6. Also B(r)/lambda^r>1+epsilon/2.
It follows that

 lambda^(2r+1)<b B(r)^2<=(1+9epsilon)lambda^(2r+1).        (20)

For the lower bound, (1-epsilon^2)(1+epsilon/2)^2
>=(1-epsilon^2)(1+epsilon)>1; for the upper bound use
(1+4epsilon)^2<=1+9epsilon.

For these three B(r),

 kappa|E|B(r)<(epsilon/2)lambda^7,
 K+kappa E B(r)>(1-4epsilon)lambda^7.                    (21)

Indeed kappa|E|<=5epsilon and B(r)<=(1+4epsilon)lambda^4,
while5(1+4epsilon)/lambda^3<1/2. Equations(18)--(21) give

 lambda^(-2)<alpha:=alpha(B_lo)<lambda^(-2)(1+20epsilon),
 1<gamma:=gamma(B_hi)<1+20epsilon,
 lambda^2<eta:=gamma(B_top)<lambda^2(1+20epsilon).         (22)

For the upper bounds, use
(1+9epsilon)/(1-4epsilon)<1+20epsilon and
(1+10epsilon)/(1-3epsilon)<1+20epsilon. The lower bounds use(20),
K<lambda^7 and E<0. In particular alpha<1. At B_hi, the equality
threshold bB^2=K+kappa E B has already been crossed. Its left side
increases and its right side decreases with B, so every larger B_j uses
the growth branch. This proves all the regime assertions in(4).

This rule also has genuinely growing rank. The valuations nu2(j) at even
indices j in1,...,N-1 take the m-4 distinct values1,...,m-4. The growth
ratios gamma(B) are strictly increasing in B, since their derivative is
(2bB-kappa E)/K>0. If the norm list had only d distinct values, it would
have at most d(d-1)/2 distinct ratios greater than1. Therefore
d(d-1)/2>=m-4. Choose one reset at each distinct scale and order them by
scale. In(5), the vector at the largest scale has a nonzero component in
a block where every smaller one vanishes. Removing it successively proves
these d vectors linearly independent. Thus dim V>=d tends to infinity.
This is an exact symbolic dimension lower bound, not a numerical rank.

The optimum-row signs used above also follow explicitly: A^2>bB_lo
because (1-epsilon)^2 lambda^4>(1+4epsilon)lambda^3. On the other hand

 bB_hi=lambda^4[(1+epsilon)(1-epsilon^2)]^2>lambda^4>A^2.

Now concentrate on cells1,2,3,4 and divide all their squared norms by t1.
The actual bridges15,23,31 have excesses B_hi,B_lo,B_top. By(4),

    t2/t1=gamma,
    t3/t1=alpha gamma,
    t4/t1=alpha gamma eta.                               (23)

In particular t3<t1<t2<t4: alpha gamma<1, gamma>1, and alpha eta>1.
The estimates in(22) also give

 1<t2/t1<1+20epsilon,
 lambda^(-2)<t3/t1<(1+50epsilon)lambda^(-2),
 1<t4/t1<1+70epsilon.                                    (24)

For example(1+20epsilon)^2<1+50epsilon and its cube is<1+70epsilon.
Set delta=1/10000. Since m>=1000000, every relative error in(17),(19),(24)
is smaller than delta. These are explicit enclosures on actual words;
no asymptotic continuity claim or numerical interval check is needed.

## 5. The old failed row is repaired, but a new original row is positive

By(2),(6),(23), the needed correlations are EXACTLY

 <v1,v2>=kappa t1, <v1,v3>=t3, <v2,v3>=kappa t3,
 <v1,v4>=kappa t1, <v2,v4>=-(7/8)t2,
 <v3,v4>=kappa t3.                                      (25)

First consider the previously offending physical pair(16,27). Equations
(7)--(10) expand it to

 D^2 Q_(16,27)
  =kappa b B_lo B_hi t1+b B_lo(A-B_lo)t2
   +[-A^2 B_hi+kappa A^2(B_lo-A)-kappa A b B_lo+A^3]t3.   (26)

Let J be the coefficient of t3. It is negative: the bounds of Section4
imply

    J/lambda^6
      <=-(1-delta)^2 lambda+1+delta/2<0,

after discarding the other negative term. Since t3/t1>lambda^-2 and
A-B_lo<0, an upper bound for(26), divided by t1, is

 kappa(1+delta)^2 lambda^6
 -(1-delta)^2 lambda^5-kappa(1-delta)^2 lambda^3
 +lambda^4+2kappa delta lambda^4.

Its value at delta=0 is lambda^4(7-3lambda)/4. The added error is less
than11delta lambda^4: use (1+delta)^2-1<=3delta,
1-(1-delta)^2<=2delta,lambda<5/2,lambda^2<25/4 and1/lambda<5/12.
Thus12/5<lambda gives

 D^2 Q_(16,27)/t1
   <lambda^4[-1/20+11/10000]<-lambda^4/25.

Because D^2<(lambda^2+lambda)^2=2lambda^4, this proves

                 Q_(16,27)<-t1/50.                     (27)

So the new mechanism actually repairs the old pair on the actual words.
It is not merely the same failed object with a different description.

Now retain the ORIGINAL pair with source16 and target35. These are both
queried indices, in cells2 and4, well before the terminal sample. They have

 g16=-(B_hi v1+A v2)/D,
 g35=-(B_top v3+A v4)/D,
 y35=(-bB_top v3+A^2 v4)/D.

Multiplying g16-g35 by y35 and using(25) yields the exact identity

 D^2 Q_(16,35)
  =[bB_top(B_hi-B_top)+kappa A^2 B_top]t3
   -kappa A^2 B_hi t1+(7/8)A^3 t2+A^3 t4.                (28)

For example the two mixed terms involving t3 and kappa combine as
kappa A b B_top+kappa A(A-b)B_top=kappa A^2 B_top.
The positive(7/8) term is the effect of the negative branch correlation.

Divide(28) by lambda^6 t1, keep every term with its sign, and apply the
finite-m enclosures. The result is bounded below by

 (1-delta)+kappa(1-delta)^2+(15/8)(1-delta)^3
       -lambda[(1+delta)^3+kappa(1+delta)].              (29)

At delta=0 this equals

       25/8-(5/4)lambda=(5/8)lambda^(-2).               (30)

For0<=delta<=1/10, the loss in the positive terms is at most57delta/8;
the extra negative magnitude is at most85delta/8. Here
(1-delta)^r>=1-r delta for r=2,3,
(1+delta)^3<=1+4delta, and lambda<5/2. The total loss is<18delta.
Consequently

 Q_(16,35) > [5/16-9delta lambda^2]t1
            >[5/16-225/40000]t1 > (3/10)t1 > 0.         (31)

This is a uniform strict violation for every actual H_m with m>=1000000.
All local adjacent and optimum rows passed, every vector is explicit,
and the complete GD recursion was retained. None of those checks can
replace the omitted global ordered row. This is an obstruction to T2,
not a counterexample to smooth-convex interpolation or to the target.

## 6. Arbitrary function values cannot rescue this exact geometry

The conclusion is stronger than a rejection of the values(9). Suppose
arbitrary values at the SAME points and gradients arose from a convex
1-smooth function having optimum sample0 with value0. Define

    phi_i=<x_i,g_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2.

The optimum row gives phi_i>=0. At each core reset x_i=g_i, the two
optimum rows require simultaneously f_i>=||g_i||^2/2 and
f_i<=||g_i||^2/2, so phi_i=0. Both orientations of an equal-gradient
pair give f_i-f_j=<g_i,x_i-x_j>; hence phi is constant on each H2 half.
In particular the entire second half of every cell has phi=0.

For arbitrary values, direct expansion gives

    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>+phi_j-phi_i.                      (32)

The geometric part of Q_(8j+4,8j+3) is zero by(11). Therefore its
necessary nonpositivity gives phi_(8j+3)<=0. Together with the optimum
condition this forces phi=0 on the first half as well. In particular
phi16=phi35=0, and the violation(31) survives EVERY value assignment.

Thus adding free conjugate heights to this exact locally maximal signed
reset law is not a repair. No unproved value-independent-cycle claim is
being substituted here: the value rigidity comes from explicit optimum,
equal-gradient and adjacent rows. Redesigning the gradients or sacrificing
local norm maximality is required before a positive height reserve exists.

## 7. A uniform neighbourhood also fails, including arbitrary values

This section is a joint extension of the same second template, not a third
extrapolation law. Retain the directions of cells1,...,4, the exact reset
positions, both constant H2 halves, all physical steps and(7). Now allow
ARBITRARY positive norms satisfying, with rho=1/1000,

    1-rho <= t2/t1 <= 1+rho,
    (1-rho)lambda^-2 <= t3/t1 <= (1+rho)lambda^-2,
    1-rho <= t4/t1 <= 1+rho.                             (33)

Do not impose equality in the local row, or any chosen function values.
There are no restrictions on earlier and later completions. The exact
law(23) lies inside(33). One still has t3<min(t1,t2,t4). Put
M14=min(t1,t4), M24=min(t2,t4). The geometric part q_geo of Q_(16,35)
is now exactly

 D^2 q_geo
  =[bB_top(B_hi-B_top)+kappa A^2 B_top]t3
   -kappa A^2 B_hi M14+(7/8)A^3 M24+A^3 t4.              (34)

Use M14<=t1, M24>=(1-rho)t1 and the actual parameter enclosures with
relative error rho. The positive terms in(34), divided by lambda^6 t1,
are at least

    (1-rho)^2+kappa(1-rho)^3+(15/8)(1-rho)^4.

The negative magnitudes are at most
lambda[(1+rho)^3+kappa(1+rho)]. The positive loss is at most41rho/4,
and the extra negative magnitude at most85rho/8. Their sum is less than
21rho. As in(30)--(31), this gives

    q_geo > [5/16-(21/2)rho lambda^2]t1
            >[5/16-525/8000]t1 > t1/5.                 (35)

The optimum and equal-gradient rows still give phi>=0 and zero phi on
every second half. At cell2 the actual local row has geometric part

 D^2 q_local=K t2+kappa E B_hi min(t1,t2)-bB_hi^2 t1.

Because E<0 and min(t1,t2)<=t1, this is at least
K[t2-gamma(B_hi)t1]. Hence its nonpositivity with arbitrary values
implies

    0<=phi16<= (K/D^2)[gamma(B_hi)t1-t2].                (36)

If the right side is negative, legality has already failed. Otherwise,
K/D^2<8: by(17)--(18), this ratio is less than
lambda^3/[2(1-rho)^2]<8, using lambda^3<125/8 and
(999/1000)^2>125/128. Equations(22),(33) give

    phi16<8(rho+20epsilon)t1< t1/100.                    (37)

Meanwhile phi35>=0. The actual row in(32) therefore satisfies

    Q_(16,35)=q_geo+phi35-phi16
                > (1/5-1/100)t1 > (3/20)t1 > 0.         (38)

This excludes ALL arbitrary value choices and ALL norms in the box(33),
not just the selected maximal norm law. To evade this neighbourhood
obstruction while retaining the reset directions and constant H2 halves,
at least one of the three ratios in(33) must leave its stated interval.
This is a necessary repair condition, not a claim that leaving the box
suffices for full interpolation or a good payoff.

There is also a precise covariance interface, without selecting another
extrapolation law. For any unit triple with <p,q_plus>=<p,q_minus>=kappa,
write zeta_branch=<q_plus,q_minus>. Nonnegativity of
||q_plus+q_minus-2kappa p||^2 gives zeta_branch>=2kappa^2-1=-7/8.
The present two-dimensional orientation triple SATURATES this bound.
Its growing total rank does not release that per-scale restriction.

If only this branch correlation is changed while the carrier, the exact
norm policy and the other inner products in(25) are retained, the new
geometric row(28) is reduced by exactly

    (zeta_branch+7/8) A^3 t2/D^2.

The same local adjacent equality still forces phi16=phi35=0. Thus a
necessary condition for any such repair is

 zeta_branch+7/8 >= D^2 Q_(16,35)/(A^3 t2) > 9/100.     (38a)

For the last bound use(31),D^2>2(1-delta)^2 lambda^4,
A^3<lambda^6,t2/t1<1+delta, and lambda^2<25/4:
the right side exceeds
(3/5)(1-delta)^2/[(1+delta)lambda^2]>
(3/5)(99/100)(4/25)>9/100.
This is only an exact necessary interface. No completed function,
all-pair legality or favorable payoff for altered branch correlations
is asserted or tested.

## 8. Complete same-word comparisons, zero cases and exact remaining scope

For positive t0 the explicit rule(4) makes every t_j positive, and(31)
excludes a legal transcript. The same is true of every completion with
positive t1 satisfying(33), by(38). If a legal degenerate completion has
t1=0, its reset at15 is a minimizer and all subsequent actual GD iterates
stay there. Its terminal objective gap is exactly zero. This argument
uses stationarity of a differentiable convex minimum, not a dimension or
an assumed initial radius. Every such completion with nonzero TRUE
initial distance therefore has normalized payoff0.

The zero case is not an unattained formal convention. Take v_j=0 for every
j, any nonzero u0, x0=D u0, use u0 on the first H2 half and gradient0 from
index4 onward. The first three steps reach(1+b)u0 and the next step1+b
reaches0. The function(15) for C=conv(0,u0) realizes these points: before
that step every point is a scalar>=1 times u0; afterwards it is0. Its
minimum set is {x:<x,u0><=0}, so the true initial distance is D||u0||>0.
This is a single legal function with every original query and payoff0.
It is the natural zero-reset degeneration of the template; the convention
u0=v0 was only the selected nonzero initial normalization in(16).

On EACH same physical word h=H_m, write S=sum h and use the COMPLETE
accepted channels, not a chosen subfamily:

    K_n(h)>=1/(1+2S)>0,
    F_ray_n(h)>=K_n(h),                                  (39)

by b98bb807d514e80d and1fca3e5dd555cb4a. For the unaugmented FULL complex
channel C_full(h)=sup_(z in D_h)Gap_h(z) of dfde2fa326c908bf, put
vartheta=1/[2(1+S)]. The scalar function vartheta*x^2/2 realizes its real
polynomial transcript, so it is in D_h. The maximal-value property of
Gap_h gives

 C_full(h)>=vartheta product_(i<n)(1-vartheta h_i)^2
              >1/[8(1+S)]>0.                            (40)

To prove the strict estimate, the nonnegative numbers vartheta h_i sum
to S/[2(1+S)]<1/2. Repeatedly applying(1-u)(1-v)>=1-u-v shows that their
product of complements exceeds1/2. This argument uses the FULL legal
complex channel, with no extra Huber term or substituted spectral lens.

Thus the only legal zero-reset members above have payoff0, strictly below
both complete K/ray references and the full complex reference. With the
ORIGINAL all-split benchmark B_n=1/U_n^ZJ from b98bb807d514e80d,

    B_n * payoff = 0                                    (41)

for these legal degenerate members, for the entire unbounded family
m>=1000000. There is no positive legal member of the specified T2 law
or its neighbourhood(33), whatever the actual true initial cost is.
The nonzero putative payoff using(16) is rejected, not reported as a bound.

This does NOT close arbitrary reset norm profiles, arbitrary changing
phase laws, all signed scale-indexed kernels, changed core returns,
within-H2 gradient changes, or general convex conjugate heights after
those geometric restrictions are relaxed. The data's internal changes
and less constrained remote covariance remain concrete missing freedoms.
Orthogonal addition of another complete transcript changes the residuals
and normalization and is not covered. Neither this obstruction nor the
finite accepted H6 witness is an upper bound on unrestricted W_n(H_m),
and even a successful H_m construction would leave the original
all-n/all-h bridge to prove.

## 9. Audit, literature and resource status

The proof is joint: physical indexing, scale realization, every local and
optimum row, both remote-row expansions, parameter enclosures, value
rigidity, the neighbourhood extension, static minimum/radius, degeneracies
and complete comparison channels are all explicit above. In particular
(27) and(31) keep their different signs and different target indices.
The neighbourhood proof uses min(t1,t4),min(t2,t4), not the ordering valid
only for the exact law. All claims remain UNVERIFIED.

Search program: active_program; mutation of a frozen-evidence-informed
signed reset construction; missing mechanism=global interpolation with
persistent signed/recurrent state. Matlas was used for polytope projection
and smooth-convex interpolation. It returned a same-face projection
statement from arXiv2106.11943 and Theorem5 of arXiv1910.01845, among
irrelevant results. Neither is a proof premise. Web search located the
primary interpolation paper arXiv1502.05666; its exact existing local text,
Sections2.4--2.5, definitions, Theorem4 and proof were read. The conjugate
reconstruction mechanism is the same one already supplied by accepted
1252b8823bb5a290. No external theorem or unverified sibling result is
imported; external_refs is empty. No further literature layer is needed
for the final row obstruction, which has reduced to this explicit
previously audited finite interpolation setting.

No solver, new trajectory/word, fit, machine algebra, proof replay,
installation, consultation, native subagent or fact_submit was used.
The only new mathematical host processing was broker job4 on frozen
inputs; the two-template ceiling is fully used(T1,T2). The diagnostic
allowance was CLOSED by main through its broker, as recorded in guidance
212480bf0e0fe4cb. Our final operational --inspect read back status closed,
matching5 inputs,4/4 jobs and0.4223282469974947 charged seconds. No duplicate
close, renewal or transfer is needed. That guidance's separate max4 formal
gate does not authorize any fact_submit by max.
The main theorem is still open. No new analytical round or resource
renewal is implied by the remaining interfaces above.
