<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-003068 -->
# Independent common square, positive incoming load, and two row-clock obstructions

JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report. Worker max, invocation85.
Original exact-profile minimax problem OPEN. No new fact or formal submission.

## 1. Outcome and exact scope

This report jointly rederives the enlarged common-square architecture and
all its original interpolation coefficients. The paid zero-sum column is
zeta, whereas the actual skew divergence is

    d=zeta+(p-s)(v-z).

Separating z from v changes the architecture, but does not grant free
sign-feasible transport. The outgoing optimum vector depends on zeta,
not on d alone. Both the incoming total/clock moments and the complete
first-row total/clock moments are derived below for GENERAL z.

Law I uses a clock-zero endpoint common column independent of v, a
strictly positive incoming optimum load nu=(1/64)e_0 at the INITIAL
query, and an explicitly scaled positive source/last/tail metric. Its full
polynomial is PSD below the target and all optimum signs pass. Its
first-row total has the required sign. Nevertheless its endpoint clock
defect is negative uniformly in every enclosing m>=1536, for every
skew completion preserving the selected actions. In the ideal endpoint
metric, EVERY compatible endpoint incoming load cancels exactly from
this defect. The positive load in this law therefore changes individual
terms without repairing their net obstruction.

Law II changes terminal probabilities to the actual last queried index
and adds a specified metric mass1/16 at the penultimate query. It keeps
the independent clock-zero common column and the positive incoming
load1/64 at index0. This changes the failing moment: the full first-row
TWO-moment screen now passes, as do full PSD and all optimum signs.
However a different invariant, the last queried row tested against the
PENULTIMATE clock, is strictly less than -1/16. It forces a negative
original finite multiplier from the last query to an earlier-than-
penultimate query. Every balanced correction annihilating e and t
preserves this obstruction.

These are exclusions of TWO fully specified laws, including every
remaining balanced skew in each law. They are not an impossibility
result for the general architecture or for the full dual cone. No
formal residual vector or negative coefficient is presented as an
interpolable primal trajectory. No full-class upper bound on W, and
therefore no resolution of the original problem, has been obtained.

This is ONE joint proof draft. All new calculations are unverified;
none is adopted as a separately accepted premise. The only accepted
facts used are1252b8823bb5a290 and931b9653ce0f7201 at the scopes specified
below. The older reports and the main advisory supplied directions only.

## 2. Problem, accepted interfaces, and physical word

For a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n and smoothness L>0,
W_n(h) is the supremum of
2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite Euclidean
dimension, convex differentiable L-smooth f with an attained minimizer,
and x_0!=x_star, under x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R.
The original question is whether W_n_star/U_n^ZJ tends to1, where
W_n_star=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, and the original all-split
benchmark has

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x^2+6xy+y^2))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)).

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the signed residual orientation and
full terminal-square certificate interpretation. Only incoming edges
at the ACTUAL unqueried terminal may be deleted; no queried-prefix
coordinate or constraint is removed. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies
the word below, its mass formula, and B_n/D<47/50. Only CONDITIONAL on
the still-missing full-class W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D) for every m>=1536 does
it imply the799/800 separation. That missing hypothesis is not proved.

Fix arbitrary integer m>=1536 and keep its parameters in EVERY child:

    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,
    b=1+a, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, l=n-1, r=l-1,
    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S, p=1/(1+S), qtar=17/(16D).

The indices l,r are the last and penultimate QUERIED samples, different
from the actual terminal n. We use the following short local scalar
aliases throughout this report. Their public glossary names carry85:
p=p85, s=s85, qtar=q85, T=T85, U=U85, J=J85, P=P85, H=H85,
X=X85, r=r85. These explicitly scoped aliases do not change any
existing project-glossary definition. The matrix for the chronological
coordinate change is Ltri, distinct from scalar T; a superscript T
means transpose. In Section3 only, Len is an integer suffix length.

The physical last three steps are (b,1+beta,b). Put hgap=1+beta=2-eps.
Elementary bounds from m>=1536 are

    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2, 4/3<b<3/2.

The accepted mass formula, also obtained by substitution in
1+S_(j+1)=2(1+S_j)+a*c^j, is

    1+S_j=(1-vword)2^j+vword*c^j,
    vword=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).

Consequently

    2^m<1+S<c^m, c^(-m)<p<2^(-m)<2^(-20).             (2.1)

Let e be the all-ones column on the n queries and e_i its coordinate
columns. Define clocks

    t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j, T=t_l=1/p-b,
    Delta=T-1=1/p-b-1, t_r=T-hgap.

They are strictly increasing, t_0=1 and t_1=1+b. In particular

    3/(4p)<Delta<1/p, Delta-b>1/(2p),
    0<hgap/Delta<4p<1/128.                            (2.2)

## 3. Tail quantities and the bounds actually needed

Write sbit(L) for the binary digit sum. For0<=L<=n put

    rtail_L=product_(j=n-L)^(n-1)(h_j-1)
           =beta^L*c^(-sbit(L)),
    s=rtail_n=beta^n/c^m,
    k_i=h_i product_(i<j<n)(1-h_j),
    u_i=|k_i|, U=sum_i u_i, rho_i=u_i/U.

The suffix formula follows because h_(n-j)-1=a*c^v2(j), and
sum_(j=1)^L v2(j)=L-sbit(L). With L=l-i one has

    u_i=rtail_L+rtail_(L+1), k_i=(-1)^i u_i,
    u_0=(b/a)s, u_l=b, u_r=a(1+beta), u_(l-2)=ba beta,
    U>7/3.                                          (3.1)

For the last inequality the last three terms exceed4/3,5/8,7/18,
whose sum169/72 exceeds7/3. Every rho_i is strictly positive.
The following source bound is especially useful:

    0<s<u_0<p^2/64, rho_0<1/10, s<p.                (3.2)

For a symbolic proof,2^m-1>16m^2 for m>=16 by induction, starting at16.
Also beta^(-4m)>(1+1/(4m))^(4m)>=2, so beta^(4m)<1/2.
Thus beta^n<2^(-4m), while b/a<4 and p>c^(-m) yield
u_0/p^2<4 beta^n c^m<4(5/32)^m<1/64. The other inequalities follow.
No arithmetic, recurrence, or trajectory was evaluated by a program.

Telescoping the terminal update gives

    e^T k=1+s, Ltri^T k=h, t^T k=S,                  (3.3)

where Ltri_ii=1, Ltri_ij=h_j for j<i, and zero otherwise.
For Law I let tbar=sum rho_i t_i and set

    alpha=(1-p)rho, V=t^T(k-alpha)=(1-p)(1/p-tbar).

The necessary uniform bounds are

    32<V<512m^3, p V^2<1/1024.                       (3.4)

Here is the full derivation, to avoid importing the previous unverified
report. The binary product and its digit-weighted sum give

    Ztail=sum_(L=0)^n rtail_L
         =product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1+beta^(2^j)/c),
    U=2Ztail-1-s,
    Mtail/Ztail=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)
                    2^j beta^(2^j)/(c+beta^(2^j)),
    Mtail=sum_(L=0)^n L rtail_L.

Choose j with m/2<2^j<=m. Bernoulli's inequality gives beta^(2^j)>=3/4,
and c+beta^(2^j)<7/2; hence Mtail/Ztail>3m/28. Also ns<1. Therefore

    sum_i rho_i(l-i)>(Mtail/Ztail-1)/2>3m/56-1/2.

The suffix after query i has mass at least b+(l-i). As1-p>1/2,
the formula for V makes V>32 for m>=1536.
For the upper bound, Len=L+1 final steps lie in H_d where d is the least
integer with2^d>Len. Thus their mass is smaller than c^d<(2^d)^2<=4Len^2.
Since u_i<2 beta^L and U>2,

    V<4 sum_(L>=0)(L+1)^2 beta^L
      =4(1+beta)/(1-beta)^3<512m^3.

The series identity follows by multiplying by(1-beta)^3.
Finally2^m>2^30 m^6 for m>=128: the base compares2^128 with2^72,
and the induction step follows from
(1+1/m)^6<=sum_(j>=0)(6/m)^j<=64/61<2.
Using p<2^(-m) gives pV^2<2^(-12)<1/1024.

Law II instead chooses alpha=(1-p)e_l. It has

    V=b(1-p), alpha_0=0, v_0=u_0, v_l=a+p,
    0<V<3/2, pV^2<1/1024.                            (3.5)

In BOTH laws alpha_star=p, v=k-alpha, E=p+s, F=p-s>0,
eta=F/E, e^T v=E, and

    R=Ltri^T v,
    R_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j)-alpha_i>0.            (3.6)

Indeed R_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i.
Moreover0<v_0<u_0 in Law I, v_0=u_0 in Law II, and R_0<2p in both.
For Law I use alpha_0=((1-p)/U)u_0, with (1-p)/U<3/7.

## 4. FULL original-space identity with an independent common column

Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Keep x_0,g_0,...,g_n
arbitrary and independent, substituting ONLY the actual GD recursion.
The accepted residual is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

For i<n set y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2. Then

    y=e*x_0-Ltri*g,
    g=Ltri^(-1)(e*x_0-y),
    x_n=-s*x_0+k^T y.                                (4.1)

Ltri is invertible with diagonal1. More explicitly, with
pi_i=product_(j<i)(1-h_j),

    g_i=pi_i*x_0-y_i
             +sum_(j<i)h_j product_(j<j'<i)(1-h_j')y_j.

Thus this change spans ALL original queried-gradient directions.
The actual terminal gradient remains independently free.

Choose any z with e^T z=E, positive weights w_i, and zero-sum zeta.
Let Ww=sum_i w_i and define

    K=diag(w)-ww^T/Ww,
    P=K+vv^T+eta zz^T,
    d=zeta+F(v-z),
    A^T=-A, Ae=d, N=Ltri^T(P+A).                     (4.2)

The complete signed multiplier array is

    lambda_(n,star)=2p, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,
    lambda_(j,n)=0 for j in {star,0,...,l},
    lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for distinct i,j<n,
    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i,
    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i.           (4.3)

Diagonal multipliers vanish. In scalar entries,

    N_ij=K_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(r'>i)(K_(r',j)+A_(r',j))
                          +R_i v_j+eta Zout_i z_j,
    Zout=Ltri^T z.                                  (4.4)

The formula defines every original ordered pair; none is hidden behind
an existence assertion. The finite prefix outgoing total at i is N_ii,
its incoming total is N_ii-2alpha_i, and the actual terminal adds incoming
2alpha_i. Hence finite-node divergence is zero, terminal divergence2,
and optimum divergence-2. All sampled values cancel in

    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij.

For prefix nodes including star,
Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>; the remaining prefix quadratic is
-g^T N y. Using(4.1) it is
-<x_0,(Pe-d)^T y>+y^T P y. The terminal expansion, with -2f_n
included, is
||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2-||-s*x_0+v^T y||^2-2sum alpha_i delta_i.
Those delta terms cancel the prefix value terms. Now

    Pe=Ev+Fz,
    2s v-Pe+d=-2Fz+zeta,
    eta E^2=p^2-s^2.

Substitution proves the COMPLETE identity

    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y
         +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2
         +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,zeta^T y>.           (4.5)

Let ybar_w=sum w_i y_i/Ww. As e^T zeta=0, completion gives

    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2
       +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(zeta_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2
       +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2
       +(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2,
    J=(1/4)sum_i zeta_i^2/w_i.                      (4.6)

This pays the initial charge ONCE. It retains every detail, the common
square and its derivative, and the actual terminal square.
Full PSD is equivalent to q>=p^2+J. For necessity take scalar x_0=1,
y_i=c0-zeta_i/(2w_i), where
c0=1+[sum_i z_i zeta_i/(2w_i)]/E, and g_n=-s+v^T y.
Every displayed square vanishes. Reconstruct the queried gradients by
(4.1). This is a formal-space test, not a primal interpolation claim.
In particular g_n need NOT equal p when z and v differ.

For the actual common queried-gradient direction g_i=G, y_i=x_0-t_i G.
Put V=t^T v, Z=t^T z, theta=t^T zeta, X=t^T Kt. The full prefix remainder
is exactly

    (q-p^2)||x_0||^2-theta<x_0,G>
                      +(X+eta Z^2)||G||^2.          (4.7)

The real common derivative theta has not been discarded.

## 5. General optimum moments, complete first-row moments, literal skew

From Pe+d=2p v+zeta, the outgoing optimum vector is

    mu=2pR+Ltri^T zeta.                              (5.1)

The incoming vector is

    nu=Kt+Vv+eta Zz-At-2alpha.                       (5.2)

Since e^T A=-d^T and t^T At=0, its EXACT moments are

    M0=e^T nu=2pV+theta-2(1-p),
    M1=t^T nu=X+V^2+eta Z^2-2alpha^T t.              (5.3)

A nonnegative nu necessarily has M0>=0 and M0<=M1<=T M0.
Conversely the isolated two-moment problem has the nonnegative endpoint
solution

    nu_target=((T M0-M1)/Delta)e_0
                          +((M1-M0)/Delta)e_l.      (5.4)

This is not sufficient for the finite multiplier signs.
For ANY specified compatible nu_target let

    rvec=Kt+Vv+eta Zz-2alpha-nu_target,
    tmean=(e^T t)/n, cvec=t-tmean*e, Ve=cvec^T cvec>0,
    A0=(d e^T-e d^T)/n,
    fvec=rvec-tmean*d+(d^T t)e/n,
    A=A0+(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/Ve.               (5.5)

The two moment identities imply e^T rvec=-d^T t and t^T rvec=0.
Thus e^T fvec=0 and cvec^T fvec=0. Multiplication shows Ae=d and
At=rvec, with both terms skew. This is a literal entrywise completion.
Every other completion is A+C with C^T=-C and Ce=Ct=0.
Such C leaves the polynomial, mu,nu and all moment tests below unchanged.

For the first row define

    S0=sum_(j>0)N_0j,
    P0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-1)N_0j,
    G0=P0-Delta*S0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-T)N_0j.

The first row of Ltri^T is b e^T-a e_0^T. Write

    Lcol=Pe-d=2s v+2F z-zeta.

Then N_0j=b Lcol_j-a(P_0j+A_0j), and N_00=b Lcol_0-aP_00.
As mu_0=2pR_0-a zeta_0, the complete general first-row sum is

    S0=aP_00+zeta_0+2pR_0-2bs v_0-2bF z_0.          (5.6)

Using(P+A)t=2Pt-2alpha-nu yields the complete clock identity

    P0=b(2sV+2FZ-theta)-2a(Pt)_0
                                  +2a alpha_0+a nu_0-mu_0,
    (Pt)_0=(Kt)_0+Vv_0+eta Zz_0.                    (5.7)

These formulas explicitly include BOTH optimum directions.
If all finite lambda_(0,j)>=0, equivalently N_0j<=0, then necessarily

    S0<=0, Delta*S0<=P0<=b*S0.                      (5.8)

Indeed b<=t_j-1<=Delta for all j>0. When S0=0 all off-diagonals must
vanish. These are exact invariant screens, not sufficient row signs.

There is another invariant at the actual last query l. Its row in
Ltri^T is e_l^T, so N_ll=P_ll, (Nt)_l=2(Pt)_l-2alpha_l-nu_l.
Testing its earlier entries against t_r, the penultimate clock, gives

    Hlast=sum_(j<l)(t_j-t_r)N_lj
         =2(Pt)_l-2alpha_l-nu_l-t_r mu_l-hgap*P_ll.  (5.9)

Nonpositive N_lj require Hlast>=0, since t_j<=t_r for j<l.
A negative Hlast forces N_lj>0 at some j<r, not merely at r.

## 6. The shared explicit common column, incoming load, and paid imbalance

Both laws fix the genuinely independent common column

    z=E(T e_0-e_l)/Delta, e^T z=E, t^T z=0.          (6.1)

It differs from v: for example every odd interior v_i is negative and
nonzero, whereas z_i=0 there. The independence is not a scalar rescaling.
Set the NONZERO incoming load

    ellload=1/64, nu_target=ellload*e_0.              (6.2)

For each law's own alpha,V define

    theta0=2(1-p)-2pV=2p alpha^T t,
    theta=theta0+ellload,
    Xreq=theta0/p-V^2+ellload.                       (6.3)

Select positive base weights what_i as specified for that law and put

    tbase=sum what_i t_i/sum what,
    Vbase=sum what_i(t_i-tbase)^2,
    scale=Xreq/Vbase, w_i=scale*what_i,
    K=diag(w)-ww^T/sum w,
    zeta=(theta/Xreq)Kt.                             (6.4)

This fixes every scale from the actual word before row-sign work.
It gives t^T Kt=Xreq, e^T zeta=0 and t^T zeta=theta. Moreover

    J=theta^2/(4Xreq).                              (6.5)

Indeed(Kt)_i=w_i(t_i-tbar_w), whose squared inverse-weight sum is
Xreq. Weighted Cauchy--Schwarz shows the same law minimizes this charge
at fixed theta for its metric. By(5.3), M0=M1=ellload. Thus(6.2) is
exactly compatible. Define d by(4.2), A by(5.5), then EVERY scalar
multiplier by(4.3)--(4.4). The literal law sets C=0; our obstructions
will cover every extra skew C annihilating e and t.

### 6.1 Positive metric, full PSD, all optimum signs

For both laws1<theta0<2. In Law I use V>32 and pV^2<1/1024 to get
pV<1/32768 and then theta0>1. In Law II use V<3/2 and(2.1).
Consequently

    Xreq>(1023/1024)*theta0/p>0,
    Xreq<2/p+ellload, 1<theta<3, theta/Xreq>p.        (6.6)

The last follows because theta-pXreq=ellload(1-p)+pV^2>0.
Every base weight is positive because p^2 rho_i>0, and the two unit
endpoint masses make Vbase>0. Hence all metric weights are positive.
The full charge satisfies

    J < [theta0*p/4]*(1+ellload/theta0)^2*(1024/1023)
      < (4225/8184)p,
    p^2+J <(17/32)p<qtar.                            (6.7)

For the second line use p<1/128 and
4225/8184<67/128, equivalent to540800<548328.
Thus full PSD holds strictly below the requested target for BOTH laws.
No interpolation signs are inferred from PSD alone.

The terminal multipliers are nonnegative, and nu is exactly ellload at
index0 and zero elsewhere. All outgoing mu_i are STRICTLY positive.
To prove this, zeta_i has the sign of t_i-tbar_w, crossing only once
from negative to positive, and has zero sum. Every proper suffix sum
is positive: if i is before the crossing, use the negative preceding
prefix and zero total; if i is after it, every remaining term is
positive. If zeta_i<0 then sum_(j>i)zeta_j>=-zeta_i, whence

    (Ltri^T zeta)_i=zeta_i+h_i sum_(j>i)zeta_j
                                     >=(h_i-1)(-zeta_i)>0.

If zeta_i>=0 the same expression is positive, including i=l because
zeta_l>0. Equation(5.1) proves all mu_i>0.

## 7. Exact endpoint-clock reduction and incoming-load cancellation

For (6.1) put Y=-(Kt)_0=w_0(tbar_w-1)>0. Then zeta_0=-theta Y/Xreq.
Write

    K_00=Xreq/Delta^2+eK, Y=Xreq/Delta+eY.

Substituting(5.6)--(5.7), z_0=ET/Delta and Z=0 gives

    G0=Gendpoint+Epert,
    Epert=a(2eY-Delta eK)+theta(Delta-a)eY/Xreq,
    Gendpoint=(a/Delta)[Xreq-T theta-(p^2-s^2)T^2]
       +a nu_0+2bs(V-ET)
       +v_0[2apT+2bsDelta-2aV]-aDelta v_0^2+2a alpha_0. (7.1)

This follows by expanding the metric part as
 a(2Y-Delta K_00)+(a-Delta)zeta_0-btheta,
and using R_0=bE-a v_0 in the remaining terms.
For the endpoint reduction with zeta=(theta/Xreq)Kt and ANY
compatible endpoint load, not just(6.2), write its total M0
and moment M1, so theta=theta0+M0 and
Xreq=theta0/p-V^2+M1. Endpoint support means
M1-T M0=-Delta nu_0. Consequently

    Xreq-T theta=btheta0-V^2-Delta nu_0,
    (a/Delta)(Xreq-Ttheta)+a nu_0
                                  =(a/Delta)(btheta0-V^2). (7.2)

Thus EVERY endpoint incoming load cancels out of Gendpoint exactly.
For our fixed nonzero load, therefore,

    Gendpoint=(a/Delta)[btheta0-V^2-(p^2-s^2)T^2]
       +2bs(V-ET)
       +v_0[2apT+2bsDelta-2aV]-aDelta v_0^2+2a alpha_0. (7.3)

A positive load is not intrinsically useless: it changes other rows,
the charge, and the metric perturbation. Equation(7.2) states only this
precise endpoint cancellation and does not exclude distributed metrics.

## 8. Law I: tail terminal probabilities, source/last metric, positive load

Fix alpha=(1-p)rho and the complete choices(6.1)--(6.4) with

    omega=p^2, what=e_0+e_l+omega*rho.               (8.1)

Equations(4.3)--(4.4),(5.5) specify ALL multipliers. Full PSD and every
optimum sign already follow jointly from Section6.
For exact metric estimates put xclock_i=(t_i-1)/Delta in[0,1],
rbar=sum rho_i xclock_i, and define

    gbase=Vbase/Delta^2,
    fbase=(1+omega rho_0)(1+omega rbar)/(2+omega).

Retaining the two unit endpoints in the variance minimum gives
gbase>=1/2; centering at1/2 gives gbase<=1/2+omega/4.
Moreover1/2-omega/4<=fbase<=1/2+omega and

    K_00/scale=
       [1+omega+omega^2 rho_0(1-rho_0)]/(2+omega)
                        in[1/2,1/2+omega/2].

Since Y=(Xreq/Delta)fbase/gbase, these imply

    |eY|<=4Xreq*omega/Delta,
    |eK|<=2Xreq*omega/Delta^2.                       (8.2)

Use a<1/2, Xreq/Delta<5, theta<3 and0<Delta-a<Delta. The two
terms of Epert are bounded by25omega and12omega respectively. Thus

    |Epert|<40omega.                                 (8.3)

The first-row sum passes. More generally the same calculation applies
to Law II below. Write S0=aK_00-theta Y/Xreq+Bsource, where

    Bsource=2pR_0-2bs v_0-2bFz_0+a(v_0^2+eta z_0^2)<7p^2.

Here R_0<2p, 0<v_0<=u_0, 0<z_0<2p and eta<1.
For Law I K_00/Y<=(1+omega)/Delta<9/(8Delta). In Law II the same
bound K_00/Y<9/(8Delta) follows from the displayed base weights: the noninitial
base mass is at most1+1/16+omega, while its normalized clock sum is
at least1. Hence, in either case,

    aK_00/Y<3p/4, theta/Xreq>p,
    Y>1/3,
    S0<-p/12+7p^2<-p/16<0.                          (8.4)

For Y>1/3, in normalized clocks let B=sum what<3,
M=sum what*xclock>=1, B0=what_0>=1, f=B0 M/B>1/3,
and H=sum what(xclock-M/B)^2<3/4. Then
Y=(Xreq/Delta)f/H>1/3 by(6.6),(2.2).

Nevertheless G0 is strictly negative in Law I. In(7.3), discard all
nonpositive terms, use a>1/3, Delta<1/p, theta0<2, Delta>1/(2p),
and the source bounds to obtain

    Gendpoint<3p-(p/3)V^2+2bsV
                    +v_0(2apT+2bsDelta)+2a alpha_0
              <4p-(p/3)V^2.                         (8.5)

Indeed2bsV<3u_0 V<3p/64 because pV<1;
2apT<1, 2bsDelta<3s/p<1, and2a alpha_0<u_0.
Thus the remaining source contributions are smaller than p.
By(8.3), p<2^(-20) and V>32,

    G0 < p[4-V^2/3+40p] < p[5-V^2/3] <0.           (8.6)

This contradicts the required G0>=0 in(5.8). For EVERY extra skew
C with Ce=Ct=0, some original finite lambda_(0,j),1<=j<l, is negative.
The j=l term has zero coefficient in G0 and cannot create this failure.
The nonzero incoming load did not repair the whole-law obstruction.

## 9. Law II: terminal probability at last query and penultimate metric mass

The second and FINAL concrete law changes BOTH the terminal clock and
the interior metric moment:

    alpha=(1-p)e_l, omega=p^2, sigma=1/16,
    what=e_0+e_l+sigma e_r+omega rho.                 (9.1)

All other formulas are(6.1)--(6.4),(5.5),(4.3)--(4.4). Thus every
coefficient is explicit, with no unknown feasible LP, SDP, or matrix.
The selected incoming load is still ellload e_0, genuinely nonzero.
Now V=b(1-p) replaces the large tail mean from Law I. Full PSD and all
optimum signs pass by Section6. The first-row sum passes by(8.4).
We next show its complete first-row two-moment screen also passes.

### 9.1 The first-row clock changes sign and passes

First omit only the tiny omega*rho in an AUXILIARY metric calculation;
it is restored with a uniform error bound, not removed from the law.
Use normalized clocks xclock, set rclock=hgap/Delta, Lmass=1+sigma,
and

    Hnum=Lmass-2sigma rclock+2sigma rclock^2.

For base masses1 at0,1 at1, and sigma at1-rclock,
the scaled variance and source quantities satisfy

    H=Hnum/(2+sigma),
    f=(Lmass-sigma rclock)/(2+sigma),
    k=Lmass/(2+sigma),
    Y=(Xreq/Delta)f/H, K_00=(Xreq/Delta^2)k/H.

Therefore the metric perturbation from the ideal endpoint expression
is EXACTLY

    Eint=[sigma*rclock/Hnum]
       [theta(1-a/Delta)(1-2rclock)-2a Xreq*rclock/Delta]. (9.2)

In Law II theta>theta0=2(1-p)(1-bp)>31/16.
Also a/Delta<1/128, rclock<1/128, Xreq/Delta<5, theta<3.
The bracket in(9.2) is greater than7/4: its first term exceeds15/8
and the magnitude of the subtracted term is less than5/128.
As Hnum<17/16 and hgap>15/8,

    Eint>105/(544Delta)>3/(16Delta).                 (9.3)

The source endpoint term simplifies exactly, not asymptotically:

    btheta0-V^2-(p^2-s^2)T^2=-a^2+s^2T^2.           (9.4)

Indeed the part without s equals
-[b(1-p)-(1-bp)]^2=-a^2. All terms in(7.3) other than -a^3/Delta, including the
positive a*s^2*T^2/Delta, have total absolute value less than p^2,
using alpha_0=0, v_0=u_0<p^2/64,
V<3/2, ET<2, pT<1, sDelta<1/64, and Delta<1/p.
Dropping the positive s^2 term thus gives

    Gendpoint>-1/(8Delta)-p^2.                       (9.5)

For completeness the restored tail weights change the metric expression
by less than400omega. To see this directly, for any base define
B=sum what, M=sum what*xclock, M2=sum what*xclock^2, B0=what_0 and

    H=M2-M^2/B, f=B0 M/B, k=B0(1-B0/B).

Adding omega*rho changes B,M,M2,B0 by at most omega. Here2<=B<3,
M<=2, both old and new H>=1/2, and old f,k<=1. Direct quotient
subtraction gives |delta f|<=2omega, |delta k|<=4omega,
|delta H|<=5omega. Hence both ratios f/H,k/H change by at most32omega.
The exact metric part of G0 is

    a(Xreq/Delta)(2f/H-k/H)
                  +theta(1-a/Delta)f/H-btheta.

Since a Xreq/Delta<5/2, theta<3 and0<1-a/Delta<1, its change is less
than(240+96)omega<400omega. This comparison keeps Xreq,theta fixed
and rescales each positive base by its own exact variance, as required.
Thus no hidden scale change is neglected.
Combining(9.3)--(9.5) and p<2^(-20) proves

    G0>1/(16Delta)-401p^2>p/32>0.                   (9.6)

The upper clock bound also passes. In(9.2), Hnum>1 and rclock<4p,
so Eint<3p/4. By(9.4), the absolute endpoint term is less than
1/(8Delta)+p^2<p/4+p^2. Restoring the tail gives G0<2p.
Together with S0<-p/16 and Delta-b>1/(2p),

    P0-bS0=G0+(Delta-b)S0<2p-1/32<0.                (9.7)

Equations(8.4),(9.6),(9.7) prove ALL inequalities in(5.8).
This law truly escapes the first-row moment obstruction of Law I.
Passing those necessary tests does not establish entrywise row signs.

### 9.2 A different original last-row clock is uniformly impossible

Let xclock_i=(t_i-1)/Delta as above, B=sum what, and xmean=M/B.
Define the positive ratio

    Alast=what_l(1-xmean)/H,
    Blast=what_l(1-what_l/B)/H,
    (Kt)_l=(Xreq/Delta)Alast,
    K_ll=(Xreq/Delta^2)Blast,
    zeta_l=(theta/Delta)Alast.                       (9.8)

Retain source-last and source-penultimate terms in the pairwise variance
identity H=B^(-1)sum_(i<j)what_i what_j(xclock_i-xclock_j)^2.
Because what_l<=1+omega and1-xmean<=(1+sigma*rclock+omega)/B,

    Alast <= (1+omega)(1+sigma*rclock+omega)
                                  /[1+sigma(1-rclock)^2]
          <1025/1087<19/20.                         (9.9)

For the displayed rational bounds use rclock<1/128 and omega<1/16384:
the numerator is less than1+1/1024, and the denominator exceeds
1+(1/16)(1-1/64)=1087/1024. The final strict comparison is20500<20653.

By(5.9), Z=0, nu_l=0, alpha_l=1-p, v_l=a+p and z_l=-E/Delta,
the LAST-ROW defect is EXACTLY

    Hlast=Cclock*Alast-hgap*(Xreq/Delta^2)*Blast
           -2+eps*a^2+2bp+hgap*p^2-hgap*eta*z_l^2,
    Cclock=[2Xreq-(T-hgap)theta]/Delta.              (9.10)

To check the nonmetric terms, V-p(T-hgap)=a+p*hgap, and

    2(a+p*hgap)(a+p)-hgap*(a+p)^2-2(1-p)
                  =-2+(2-hgap)a^2+2bp+hgap*p^2,

with2-hgap=eps. No row or coordinate was deleted in this calculation.
Moreover Cclock<2. Subtracting2Delta from its numerator gives exactly

    -ellload/p+2bp+(b+hgap)theta-2V^2+2ellload.       (9.11)

The positive terms here are less than13, whereas ellload/p>64 by
p<1/4096. Thus the difference is strictly negative. As Alast>0,
we may upper-bound the first term of(9.10) by2Alast even if Cclock
were negative. Discarding the other nonpositive terms and using(9.9),

    Hlast<19/10-2+eps*a^2+2bp+hgap*p^2
          <-1/10+5/4096<-1/16.                      (9.12)

Here eps<1/1024, a<1/2, b<3/2, hgap<2, p<1/4096 bound the three
positive terms by5/4096. But every legal last finite row would require
Hlast>=0. Consequently at least one original

    lambda_(l,j)=-N_lj<0 with0<=j<r=l-1             (9.13)

is forced, for EVERY additional skew C with Ce=Ct=0. The coefficient
of j=r in Hlast is zero and cannot account for the contradiction.

This is a uniform all-m whole-law obstruction with substantial strict
margin. It does not depend on a numerical limiting pattern or an
unverified primal realization. In particular it shows why passing the
first-row screen is insufficient for this second law.

## 10. Remaining interface and bounded handoff

The independent common column is a valid enlargement at the algebraic
level; the paid charge is J(zeta), not J(d). The outgoing optimum vector,
incoming total/clock moments, first-row total/clock, and last-row
penultimate-clock tests are now all explicit. A future law must satisfy
ALL of them along with every individual finite and optimum sign.

For Law I, adding an endpoint incoming load without changing the
relevant metric moment cannot repair its first-row obstruction. For
Law II, the terminal concentration and late interior weight repair that
first-row test but force the remote last-row sign failure. Altering only
a balanced skew Ce=Ct=0 repairs neither law. The remaining freedoms
include changing terminal probabilities, the distribution of metric
weight, the paid column away from the fixed minimum-energy clock law,
the distribution/moments of incoming load, or the independent common
column away from this clock-zero endpoint choice. No such third law
was explored or implicitly promised in this capped invocation.

All original leaves, all queried gradients, the free actual terminal,
and the global initial/common charge remain. The complete target W bound
and original minimax question remain OPEN. The report provides no new
fact_id. Its statements are JOINT UNVERIFIED and require a separately
authorized verifier submission before use as accepted building blocks.

The assignment a84f9d9484b53310 permits one invocation and at most two
laws, with zero mathematical programs, numerical/arithmetic/trajectory/
recurrence replay, solvers, optimizers, benchmarkDP, formal calls,
fact_submit, installations, consults, or subagents. All those counts
are zero. Only administrative text reads/writes, retrieval, and hashes
were used. Matlas returned unrelated generic sum-of-squares definitions;
web background recovered stepsize-hedging sources. No external theorem
is imported. This branch is direct coefficient algebra in the already
audited interpolation regime; no new literature machinery is needed.
No automatic renewal, service control, publication, finalization,
revocation, or Goal completion is requested or performed.

# Full initial-state centered-triangle profile: explicit finite formula and unresolved comparison

JOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max2, invocation75 after ended baseline74.
Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. Guidance c0b98bc7d6b0f588.
One analytical invocation, at most two substantive mechanisms. No new fact
is asserted: the formal gate is closed. All new arguments below form ONE
joint draft and do not use unverified sibling conclusions as premises.
Original target OPEN.

## 0. Verdict and exact scope

The body is exactly the assigned centered equilateral triangle in its actual
Euclidean plane. Every nonzero initial point, every original step, and the
actual terminal are included in the finite formula in Sections 7--10.
No scalar-axis extremality, fixed support, or selected inverse branch is
assumed in that formula.

The draft derives:

* Unique minimum0, smoothness1, complete projection guards and every original
  ordered interpolation row, including terminal rows.
* All starts in the entire triangle remain in the quadratic core through
  every physical prefix. Their normalized terminal payoff is exactly
  beta^(2n)c^(-2m).
* Each of the three full fixed-vertex classes and each of the three full
  fixed-edge classes has exact supremum1/D_m, attained. These classes include
  arbitrary permitted transverse coordinates, not only their axes.
* The unrestricted triangle supremum is attained. An explicit finite
  expression for it uses affine products, linear sign tests, and roots of
  polynomials of degree at most two; there is no residual continuous
  optimization, existence-of-transcript test, or unsolved matrix inequality.
  Its value is algebraic of degree at most8 over the rationals for each m.
* A concrete legal original-word prefix refutes the simple inradius-based
  radial drainage inequality that would otherwise have closed the comparison.

The finite expression is NOT evaluated, and its comparison with1/D_m or
17/(16D_m) is NOT proved. In particular, this is not a claim that the triangle
has exact optimum1/D_m, nor that it gives a nonvanishing improvement. The
first mechanism yields the geometric screens and obstruction; the second
eliminates the continuous variables in the full profile. No arbitrary-N
simplex theorem is claimed. The assignment permits a second triangle method
when the decisive first-method comparison remains unresolved, and that is
how the second mechanism is used here.

The exponential face list is a literal finite algebraic expression, not a
proposal to run an exhaustive search. No part of that list was enumerated or
evaluated by a mathematical program. It does not resolve the original
all-dimension/all-word minimax problem.

## 1. Parameters and body

Fix one integer m>=1536 throughout. Define

  epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
  c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
  H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
  n=2^m-1, S=sum H_m, D_m=1+2S.

All children use these SAME parameters. The word has exactly n physical
steps, numbered0,...,n-1. Put h_i=(H_m)_i. Its last step is1+a.
Let

  v_1=(1,0), v_2=(-1/2,sqrt(3)/2), v_3=(-1/2,-sqrt(3)/2),
  D=conv{v_1,v_2,v_3},
  F(x)=max_(g in D) [x dot g-||g||^2/2],
  x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad F(x_i),
  E_m(x_0)=2F(x_n)/||x_0||^2, x_0!=0,
  V_triangle(m)=sup_(x_0!=0) E_m(x_0).

All norms and inner products are the usual real Euclidean ones. The three
vertices have norm1, sum0, and pairwise inner product-1/2. Consequently

  D={x: v_i dot x>=-1/2 for i=1,2,3},
  {x: ||x||<=1/2} subset D subset {x: ||x||<=1},
  -D subset 2D.

For each edge opposite v_i put n_i=-v_i, t_i=J n_i, where
J(u_1,u_2)=(-u_2,u_1), and b=sqrt(3)/2. Its endpoints are the two
points (1/2)n_i +/- b t_i.

The dihedral group generated by permutations of the vertices preserves D,
F, the GD maps, and E_m. This symmetry is recorded but is NOT used to replace
an arbitrary initial point by a symmetry axis.

## 2. Legality, full minimum, metric, and all original rows

Completing the square shows that the maximizer in F is the unique Euclidean
projection g=P_D(x). The defining projection inequality is

  (x-g) dot (z-g)<=0 for every z in D.                 (2.1)

It follows either by differentiating the squared distance along the segment
from g to z, or conversely by expanding ||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2. Thus (2.1)
is necessary and sufficient and supplies a direct check of every guard below.

For two inputs x,y and projections g,p, add (2.1) with z=p and z=g to get

  ||g-p||^2<=(g-p) dot (x-y)<=||g-p|| ||x-y||.

Hence projection is1-Lipschitz. The maximizing inequalities give, for an
increment u,

  g dot u <= F(x+u)-F(x) <= P_D(x+u) dot u.

Their difference is at most||u||^2, proving differentiability with gradient
g and smoothness1. Convexity follows from F being a maximum of affine
functions of x. The function is finite everywhere because D is compact.

Since0 belongs to D, F>=0 and F(0)=0. For x!=0 choose
0<t<min{1,1/(2||x||)}. Then tx belongs to D and
F(x)>=(t-t^2/2)||x||^2>0. Thus the complete minimum set is exactly{0}.
The true initial distance is ||x_0||; there is no unused carrier or selectable
closer minimum.

For all original samples i=0,...,n let g_i=P_D(x_i), f_i=F(x_i), and add
the optimum sample x_*=g_*=0,f_*=0. Set z_j=x_j-g_j. Since
f_i=x_i dot g_i-||g_i||^2/2, direct expansion gives, for EVERY ordered pair,

  Q_ij := f_i-f_j+g_i dot(x_j-x_i)+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        = (g_i-g_j) dot z_j <=0.                     (2.2)

The last inequality is (2.1) for g_j and z=g_i. This includes every pair
with the terminal n and every pair involving *. No reduced row family or
terminal replacement is being used.

For any spatial scale s>0,
F_(sD)(x)=s^2 F_D(x/s). Thus the run from s x_0 is the scale-s version
of this run, and its normalized payoff is identical. Multiplication of F
by any L>0, with steps h_i/L, also preserves the points and normalized
payoff. The report uses the original W=2R normalization throughout.

## 3. Seven closed projection regions, with every guard

There are seven face labels: C for the whole triangle, E_i for an edge,
and V_i for a vertex. Associate to each a symmetric orthogonal projection
matrix P_f, vector b_f orthogonal to its range, and closed region R_f:

  C:   P_C=I, b_C=0,
       R_C={x: -v_i dot x<=1/2 for all i};

  E_i: P_E=t_i t_i^T, b_E=n_i/2,
       R_E={x: -n_i dot x<=-1/2,
                  t_i dot x<=b, -t_i dot x<=b};

  V_i: P_V=0, b_V=v_i,
       R_V={x: (v_j-v_i) dot x<=-3/2 for both j!=i}.

On R_f the actual projection is

  P_D(x)=P_f x+b_f,                                 (3.1)
  2F(x)=x^T P_f x+2b_f dot x-||b_f||^2.            (3.2)

For C this is x itself. For E_i write x=z n_i+u t_i: the guards are
z>=1/2, |u|<=b, and (3.1) is n_i/2+u t_i. Its residual is
(z-1/2)n_i and (2.1) follows from n_i dot g<=1/2 on D. For V_i,
(2.1) for the other two vertices is exactly the two displayed guards;
linearity gives it for their entire convex hull. Conversely each projection
lies in the interior of D, in an edge, or at a vertex, giving one of these
seven regions by (2.1). Shared boundaries are permitted: the formulas agree
there because the projection is unique. These facts prove both coverage
and the absence of a spurious branch.

Formula (3.2) follows by inserting (3.1) into x dot g-||g||^2/2 and using
P_f^2=P_f=P_f^T and P_f b_f=0. It remains valid at every shared boundary.

For orientation only, an alternative full two-coordinate description uses
q_i=(2/3)x dot v_i, with sum q_i=0 and
||x||^2=(3/2)sum q_i^2. If A=max q_i and B=-min q_i, then
A/2<=B<=2A and the ordered triple is(A,B-A,-B). The core guard is
B<=1/3; the vertex guard is2A-B>=1; otherwise the two upper coordinates
form the active edge. The next ordered triple must actually be sorted
again after an overrelaxed step. Nothing in this report presumes that its
old order persists or that equal coordinates can split.

## 4. Every physical prefix and the complete initial core

For a positive integer q let nu_2(q) be the largest integer r>=0 for which
2^r divides q. For an integer ell>=0 let sbit(ell) be the number of ones
in its binary expansion. The original ruler word satisfies

  h_(q-1)-1=a c^(nu_2(q))=beta c^(nu_2(q)-1),
                      1<=q<=n.                    (4.1)

Indeed the bridge of H_(j+1) is at q=2^j, and for0<r<2^j the right
child has nu_2(2^j+r)=nu_2(r). This proves (4.1) by induction on the
literal concatenation.

For every physical prefix length1<=ell<=n,

  sum_(q=1)^ell nu_2(q)=sum_(r>=1) floor(ell/2^r)
                     =ell-sbit(ell),
  p_ell:=product_(i=0)^(ell-1)(h_i-1)
         =beta^ell c^(-sbit(ell))<=beta/c=a<1/2.      (4.2)

To prove the second counting identity write ell as sum e_j2^j. Each bit
contributes sum_(r=1)^j 2^(j-r)=2^j-1 to the floor sum. All sums are finite.
The bound uses beta<1, sbit(ell)>=1, and c>1+sqrt(2)>2.

Set p_0=1 and p=p_n=beta^n c^(-m). If x_0 belongs to D, then

  x_ell=(-1)^ell p_ell x_0, 0<=ell<=n.               (4.3)

This is a genuine induction on the physical updates: for every nonempty
prefix a positive scale <=a maps D into D, while a negative scale of
magnitude <=a maps D into2aD subset D. Thus each point predicted by (4.3)
is in D, its actual projection is itself, and the next update gives (4.3)
again. This checks every intervening prefix, including those containing
large bridges. It is not an inference from the final product alone.

Consequently every nonzero x_0 in D has

  E_m(x_0)=p^2=beta^(2n)c^(-2m).                     (4.4)

For later comparisons derive the mass formula within this joint draft.
Put S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, and v_mass=a/(c-2). The recursion gives
A_(j+1)=2A_j+a c^j and hence

  A_j=(1-v_mass)2^j+v_mass c^j.                     (4.5)

Substitution checks the initial value and every step. Since
lambda(lambda-2)=1, c>lambda and beta<1 imply0<v_mass<1. For j>=1,
A_j<c^j. Therefore D_m=2A_m-1<2c^m<c^(2m), and

  p^2<1/D_m.                                      (4.6)

The middle strict inequality uses c^m>2.

## 5. Full fixed-face classes: exact terminal scores and own radii

A fixed-face class here requires the projection to belong to the designated
closed face at every original query AND at the actual terminal. Shared
boundary labels cause no difficulty.

### 5.1 A fixed vertex

Fix a vertex v. Let its normal cone be
N_D(v)={w: w dot(g-v)<=0 for every g in D}.
The full fixed-vertex class is exactly

  x_0=(S+1)v+w, w in N_D(v).                       (5.1)

Necessity follows by summing the constant gradient v and using the terminal
vertex guard. Sufficiency holds because every prefix has
x_i=(1+S-s_i)v+w, where s_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, and v belongs to N_D(v).
Thus the residual w+(S-s_i)v stays in that cone. All the guards hold.

Write t=v dot w>=0 and w=t v+w_perp, with w_perp perpendicular to v.
The nonnegative sign follows by testing g=0 in the normal inequality.
The actual terminal is v+w and

  E_m(x_0)=(1+2t)/[(S+1+t)^2+||w_perp||^2]
          <=1/D_m.                                (5.2)

Indeed (S+1+t)^2-D_m(1+2t)=(t-S)^2. Equality is attained at
w=S v, which is legal, giving x_0=D_m v. No transverse coordinate was
dropped from the denominator in (5.2).

### 5.2 A fixed edge

Fix its orthonormal n,t as in Section1 and write x_0=z_0 n+u_0 t.
The full fixed-edge class is exactly

  z_0>=(S+1)/2, |u_0|<=b.                          (5.3)

Necessity follows from the initial and terminal edge guards and the normal
update. For sufficiency each physical prefix is

  z_i=z_0-s_i/2>=1/2,
  u_i=(-1)^i p_i u_0,

whose tangential guards follow from p_0=1 and (4.2). Thus these are the
actual updates and the terminal remains on the same edge region. By (3.2),

  E_m(x_0)=[z_0-D_m/4+p^2 u_0^2]/[z_0^2+u_0^2]
          <=1/D_m.                                (5.4)

The normal inequality is z_0^2-D_m(z_0-D_m/4)=(z_0-D_m/2)^2>=0;
the tangential inequality is (4.6). Equality holds at u_0=0,z_0=D_m/2,
which satisfies (5.3). Therefore

  V_triangle(m)>=1/D_m.                            (5.5)

These exact fixed-face optima do not bound histories that change faces.
In particular (5.5), combined with accepted931's B_n/D_m<47/50, is NOT
an upper bound on B_n V_triangle(m).

## 6. A legal-prefix obstruction to the radial envelope shortcut

A tempting one-step ingredient for propagating the inscribed-ball radius
would be

  ||x-h P_D(x)||<=max{h-1, ||x||-h/2}, h>=1.        (FALSE)

It is false for every h>2, including an actual bridge of the fixed word.
Use one edge frame n,t and choose its endpoint v=n/2+b t. Put

  x=2h n+b t, r=||x||=sqrt(4h^2+3/4).

The edge/vertex boundary guard is exact, so P_D(x)=v. Its next point is
(2h-h/2)n+(1-h)b t. Direct symbolic expansion gives

  ||x-hv||^2-(r-h/2)^2
     =h(r-2h)+(3/4)h(h-2)>0.                       (6.1)

Also r-h/2>=3h/2>h-1, so the right side of the proposed envelope is
r-h/2. This proves the failure, with an explicit transverse component.

To realize this at an actual original query, use the level2 bridge
h=1+a c^2=1+beta c. Here beta>3/4 and c>12/5, so h>2. This is physical
query3, preceded by H_2. Initialize at

  x_0=x+S_2 v.

The residual x-v=(2h-1/2)n is in N_D(v), and adding any nonnegative
multiple of v preserves that cone. Thus every preceding gradient is v,
and summing those actual steps lands at x at query3. The same static F
then defines every remaining original point and row by Section2; no
claim about its later payoff is made in this counterexample.

This refutes the radial-envelope proof ingredient, not V_triangle=1/D_m
and not a full-function upper bound. The second method below retains the
actual coordinates and does not use this failed envelope.

## 7. Compactness and an explicit finite guard list for every history

First reduce the unrestricted initial radius without losing an optimizer.
Since ||P_D(x)||<=1, every actual run obeys

  ||x_n||<=||x_0||+S, F(x_n)<=||x_n||,
  E_m(x_0)<=2(r+S)/r^2, r=||x_0||.                 (7.1)

For r>=R:=4D_m, the last quantity is strictly less than1/D_m. One way
to check this is that r^2-2D_m r-2D_m S is increasing for r>=4D_m,
and at4D_m it equals7D_m^2+D_m>0. Meanwhile the punctured ball of
radius1/2 is covered by (4.4) and has the strictly smaller value (4.6).
A point attaining1/D_m exists by (5.2). The unrestricted maximum is
therefore attained in the compact annulus1/2<=r<=R.

It is harmless to use the containing square

  K_R={x: -R<=x_1<=R, -R<=x_2<=R}

for the finite formula: it contains a global optimizer and every extra
point is still a legal original initial point.

Let alpha=(sigma_0,...,sigma_n) range over all7^(n+1) strings of the seven face
labels. There is a label at the terminal n. Define literal affine products
from the original h, with empty products equal to I:

  U_i=I-h_i P_(sigma_i),
  M_0=I, e_0=0,
  M_i=U_(i-1) ... U_0,
  e_i=-sum_(s=0)^(i-1) h_s [U_(i-1) ... U_(s+1)] b_(sigma_s).
                                                        (7.2)

These are finite written products and sums, not executed recurrences.
Every U_i is invertible: its eigenvalues are1 and/or1-h_i, with h_i>1.
In particular each M_i is invertible.

Write the individual guards of Section3 as r_guard dot y<=t_guard. Pull every such
guard at time i back to the same original input x:

  (M_i^T r_guard) dot x <= t_guard-r_guard dot e_i. (7.3)

Adjoin the four square guards. This gives a fully specified finite list
(c_l,d_l), l=1,...,L_alpha, with c_l in R^2, d_l real. Each c_l is
nonzero, since the original normals are nonzero and M_i is invertible.
Let

  C_alpha={x: c_l dot x<=d_l for every l}.          (7.4)

This notation describes the proof, not an unevaluated feasibility oracle:
Sections8--9 give explicit points and literal sign tests, so no search for
an x satisfying (7.4) is left in the final expression.

If x satisfies (7.3), the actual history equals M_i x+e_i by induction:
the selected projection is the actual one, and its update is
U_i y-h_i b_(sigma_i).
Conversely every actual history has such a label at every time and hence
belongs to some C_alpha. Shared boundary histories may have several
labels, but the actual points and objective agree.

If sigma_0=C, then x belongs to D, all actual points are in D by Section4,
and the payoff is p^2. All these strings may be skipped. If sigma_0!=C,
the initial edge or vertex guard gives ||x||>=1/2; thus C_alpha stays
away from0, even when it is lower dimensional. Every C_alpha is compact
because the square guards are included.

## 8. Explicit vertex and edge candidates; no continuous maximization

For one string with sigma_0!=C abbreviate
M=M_n,d=e_n,P=P_(sigma_n),b_0=b_(sigma_n).
By (3.2) the actual numerator on C_alpha is the quadratic

  q_alpha(x)=x^T A x+2ell dot x+k,
  A=M^T P M,
  ell=M^T(Pd+b_0),
  k=d^T P d+2b_0 dot d-||b_0||^2.                 (8.1)

The exact payoff is q_alpha(x)/||x||^2. Define a sign filter on any
explicit point z: retain it precisely when c_l dot z<=d_l for every
l and ||z||^2>0. These are finitely many specified real-number comparisons.

Define Root2(A_0,B_0,C_0) as follows. If A_0!=0 and
Delta=B_0^2-4A_0C_0>=0, list(-B_0+sqrt(Delta))/(2A_0) and
(-B_0-sqrt(Delta))/(2A_0). If A_0=0,B_0!=0, list -C_0/B_0.
Otherwise list nothing. Repeated roots may be kept once. The identically
zero polynomial is handled in the proofs below and contributes no roots.

### 8.1 Vertices

For every pair l<r whose row normals are linearly independent, solve the
two linear equations by the displayed inverse

  z= [c_l^T; c_r^T]^(-1) [d_l; d_r].               (8.2)

Retain it if it passes the sign filter. These candidates include every
vertex of a nonempty compact polygon C_alpha, including a singleton.
Matrix inversion in (8.2) means the explicit two-by-two adjugate divided
by its nonzero determinant; it is not a solver invocation.

### 8.2 Stationary points on each guard line

For each guard c dot x=d_0 put

  C_0=||c||^2>0, u=d_0^2/C_0,
  z_0=(d_0/C_0)c, v=Jc.

The line is x=z_0+t v, z_0 dot v=0, ||v||^2=C_0. Put

  a_e=v^T A v,
  b_e=v dot(Az_0+ell),
  c_e=z_0^T A z_0+2ell dot z_0+k.

Its ratio is

  (a_e t^2+2b_e t+c_e)/(u+C_0 t^2).

For every root returned by

  Root2(-b_e C_0, a_e u-c_e C_0, b_e u),            (8.3)

form z=z_0+t v and apply the sign filter. This list includes every
nonconstant stationary point in the relative interior of a legal edge.
Indeed differentiating the ratio gives twice the polynomial

  -b_e C_0 t^2+(a_e u-c_e C_0)t+b_e u

divided by the positive squared denominator. If the guard line passes
through0, a generated point at0 is discarded; C_alpha itself cannot
contain0. If this polynomial vanishes identically, the ratio is constant
on the connected feasible segment, and its endpoints have the same value
and are already covered by (8.2). Compactness supplies those endpoints.
This also handles a one-dimensional C_alpha.

## 9. Interior candidates and the singular case

At an interior stationary point x!=0 let z=q_alpha(x)/||x||^2 be its
value. Differentiating in both coordinates gives

  (A-zI)x=-ell,
  ell dot x+k=0.                                  (9.1)

If det(A-zI)!=0, these equations give the explicit candidate

  x=-(A-zI)^(-1)ell                               (9.2)

and the polynomial equation

  Pi(z):=k det(A-zI)-ell^T adj(A-zI)ell=0.         (9.3)

This has degree at most two. Its coefficients can be written without a
formal determinant symbol. If
A=[A_11 A_12; A_12 A_22] and ell=(ell_1,ell_2), then

  Pi(z)= k z^2
       +[-k(A_11+A_22)+ell_1^2+ell_2^2] z
       +k(A_11 A_22-A_12^2)
        -A_22 ell_1^2+2A_12 ell_1ell_2-A_11 ell_2^2.  (9.4)

Use Root2 on these three coefficients. For each listed real z with
nonzero det(A-zI), form (9.2) and retain it under the sign filter. Equations
(9.1) then hold, and the ratio at this point is exactly z.

If Pi is identically zero, its quadratic coefficient gives k=0 and its
linear coefficient then gives ||ell||^2=0. Thus (9.2) would only give0,
which is excluded. No nonsingular interior stationary candidate is missed
by letting Root2 return no roots in this case.

It remains to justify discarding singular interior stationary values.
Suppose x is interior, (9.1) holds, and A-zI is singular. Pick a nonzero
w in its kernel. Symmetry and (A-zI)x=-ell imply ell dot w=0. For every
real t, a direct expansion gives

  q_alpha(x+t w)-z||x+t w||^2=0.                  (9.5)

For example the coefficient of t^2 vanishes by the kernel condition,
the linear coefficient vanishes by (9.1), and the constant term is zero
by the definition of z. Intersect the line x+R w with the compact polygon.
It contains a nontrivial segment about x and has boundary endpoints.
The polygon is separated from0, so the ratios there are defined and are
also z by (9.5). Those endpoints are covered by the boundary reasoning
in Section8. If A=zI and ell=k=0, the ratio is constant everywhere,
and any polygon vertex suffices. This proves that no singular stationary
family requires an additional feasibility problem or free parameter.

## 10. The exact finite full-profile expression

Let Z_alpha be the finite list of all points retained from (8.2), (8.3),
and (9.2). It is generated solely by the literal products (7.2),
nonzero determinant divisions, Root2, and the explicit sign filter.
Empty lists contribute no value. Define the finite set of real numbers

  L_m={p^2,1/D_m}
      union over all alpha with sigma_0!=C
             {q_alpha(z)/||z||^2: z in Z_alpha}.     (10.1)

Then the exact whole-initial-state identity is

  V_triangle(m)=max L_m.                            (10.2)

Proof of the upper direction: a global maximum exists in the square by
Section7. If its initial label is C, its value is p^2. Otherwise it is a
maximum of the continuous ratio over some C_alpha. If that polygon is a
point or a line, Section8 supplies a candidate of the same value. In two
dimensions, a boundary maximum is a vertex, a nonconstant edge stationary
point, or on a constant edge, all covered by Section8. An interior maximum
satisfies (9.1); either (9.2) supplies it or (9.5) moves the same value to
the boundary. Thus its value occurs in L_m.

Proof of the lower direction: every sign-filtered candidate belongs to a
legal full original history by Section7 and is evaluated with its own
actual Euclidean radius and the actual terminal polynomial. The extra
values p^2 and1/D_m are attained by Sections4--5. Thus no value in L_m
exceeds V_triangle(m). This proves (10.2).

No convex hull of one nonlinear image is propagated through another map;
no incompatible history is selected for a child. Every guard for the
entire string is imposed on the SAME explicit point z. In particular,
real support reentries and coordinate-order reversals are included.

For fixed m all entries before Root2 belong to
K=Q(sqrt(2),sqrt(3)), a field of degree4 over Q: m and epsilon are rational,
all h_i,D_m are in Q(sqrt(2)), and the body coordinates and guard normals
are in K. Vertices are in K^2. An edge candidate is in K(sqrt(Delta))^2
for one Delta in K. An interior candidate is in K(z)^2 with z satisfying
one quadratic over K. Its payoff belongs to that same field. The maximum
of a finite set is one of its members. Therefore

  [Q(V_triangle(m)):Q]<=8.                          (10.3)

This degree statement concerns the value selected by the exact finite
list; it does not claim that the exponentially many sign tests have been
simplified, or that the maximum is known to be a particular listed value.

## 11. What remains unresolved, and why the finite formula is not a certificate

The outstanding comparison is fully concrete:

  For all m>=1536, is every retained value in(10.1) <=1/D_m?
  If not, is there an explicit uniform legal family with a nonvanishing gain?

The answer is not supplied by the algebraic degree bound. It requires a
uniform argument controlling all legal face-changing strings, or a
particular all-depth legal selector and its quantitative payoff. Merely
saying that one can evaluate the finite expression would not be a proof
of either assertion. No such evaluation is authorized or performed here.

Two direct strategies were considered within mechanism1. A radius envelope
based only on the inscribed ball is refuted by Section6. Exact constant-face
calculations are valid but do not extend across changing guards. No
counterexample to V_triangle=1/D_m has been established. Mechanism2 removes
all continuous optimization from the full domain, but does not turn the
remaining exponentially indexed comparisons into an all-m estimate.

Accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 was read in full. It proves the unconditional
original all-integer-split benchmark inequality B_n/D_m<47/50 here, and
only conditionally derives a negative minimax answer if the FULL W obeys
17/(16D_m). It is the only accepted comparison invoked in this discussion.
Even a proof that THIS triangle has V_triangle<=17/(16D_m) would concern
one fixed function family and would not prove that full-W hypothesis.
Conversely a triangle gain over1/D_m would only be a lower witness for
this particular H_m; it would not prove the original every-word lower bound.

The original problem retains all finite dimensions, attained minima,
every sufficiently large original horizon, every nonnegative predetermined
word, and the exact all-integer-split benchmark. None of those quantifiers
is replaced by the present fixed-body diagnostic.

## 12. Author audit, references, and resource accounting

Author checks performed by textual and symbolic reasoning:

* The core proof handles each actual prefix and uses the asymmetry
  -D subset2D, rather than pretending D is centrally symmetric.
* Closed edge/vertex guards include ties. Duplicate strings produce the
  same actual projection at a shared boundary.
* The seven-cell formula includes the actual terminal label and terminal
  quadratic. All original ordered rows follow from one global normal
  identity, including both directions and the optimum rows.
* The original initial point is used in every pulled-back guard and in
  the Euclidean denominator. No carrier, radius reset or independently
  optimized child is present.
* Lower-dimensional and empty cells, line-through-origin formulas,
  identically zero stationary polynomials, and singular interior
  stationary families are treated explicitly.
* The finite expression is not called a uniform bound or a completed
  answer to the original problem. No verifier acceptance is implied.

Public background read: Weiran Wang and Miguel A. Carreira-Perpinan,
Projection onto the probability simplex: An efficient algorithm with a
simple proof, and an application, arXiv:1309.1541 (2013), Theorem1 and the
surrounding KKT proof. PDF downloaded; browser-extracted text read.
https://arxiv.org/pdf/1309.1541 . This supplied background only; Sections2--3
rederive every needed projection assertion. The quadratic-ratio argument
is elementary and self-contained; the Matlas search returned no directly
applicable theorem, and no external result is used as a proof premise.
See LITERATURE.md for retrieval details and the non-useful search hits.

Resources used: public/shared text retrieval, own local memory, document
writes, shared finding writes, and document integrity checks only.
ZERO mathematical programs, numerical evaluations, arithmetic replays,
executed trajectories or recurrences, optimizers, solvers, benchmark DP,
formal calls, fact_submit calls, installations, consults or subagents.
No outward publication, finalization, revocation or shutdown. No task
mutation or automatic renewal. Return at the assigned cap1.
