# Exact absorption of the actual two-Huber ruler tail

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No fact_submit was
allocated or called; there is no new fact_id. A mathematical statement in
this document is a candidate with its full proof, not verifier-accepted
truth. The original all-word/all-horizon minimax problem remains OPEN.

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; one allocated round after
completed56, completed ceiling57 / attempted ceiling58. Assignment TASK.md;
guidance5f75454952cf10c5; plan8ad15c87bcebe740. No automatic renewal.
Latest guidance reread7b252271b54d4ab9 leaves this assignment unchanged.

Frozen source candidate: shared finding4c4a21a484564bdc, complete public
text research/shared_evidence/4c4a21a484564bdc.md, 39298 bytes,
SHA25607140ce4efba7c8237ae2dfff447fe2afbadbbe94154a82db82d504640f2b79b.
It was read in full. It is UNVERIFIED and is not an accepted predecessor.
All construction, smoothness, trajectory, local-bound and radius ingredients
used here are proved jointly below. Accepted fact04219554e41d9750 is used
ONLY for the original benchmark ray limit and its stated enclosure.

## 1. The resulting theorem and its exact scope

Put lambda=1+sqrt(2), d=lambda-2=1/lambda. For integer m>=32 and

    1/5 <= tau <= 1/4,       8/m <= beta <= 1/4,

use the SAME enclosing parameters throughout the physical word:

    eta_m=1-tau/m, c_m=lambda(1+tau/m), a_m=eta_m/c_m,
    b_j=1+a_m c_m^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, n_m=2^m-1, J=m-2, L=m-1.             (1.1)

Here beta is beta_arc, not eta_m. Select ONLY the first occurrences of
levels m-2 and m-1. The entire physical word is exactly

    H_m=(H_J,b_J,H_J,b_(J+1),H_(J+1)).                (1.2)

Use the direct circular construction in Sections2--4, unchanged from the
frozen candidate. This is an actual convex1-smooth function F on R^3,
initialized at e_1, and all steps are physical GD steps x^+=x-h grad F.
Write E_m(tau,beta)=2F(x_(n_m))/dist(e_1,argmin F)^2.

There is one finite integer m_0 such that for EVERY m>=m_0 and EVERY
(tau,beta) in the displayed rectangle, the two old circular components
are both exactly zero at the actual FINAL iterate. In fact the old pair
enters its moving flat interval by the end of H_(m-1) and thereafter is
stationary. No tail step, old component or radius term is discarded.
Consequently its full actual payoff is exactly

    E_m(tau,beta)=D_3^2/[2(1+S_(m-1)) R_true^2],      (1.3)

with D_3 and R_true defined in Sections3--4.

Let B_n be the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark in Section8, and let the
positive finite Z_1=lim_m B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m be supplied by accepted
fact04219554e41d9750. Set C_beta=beta+sqrt(1+2beta). Uniformly on the
same moving parameter rectangle,

    B_(n_m) E_m(tau,beta)
       - Z_1 exp(-tau)/(2 lambda C_beta^4) -> 0.      (1.4)

In particular, for every FIXED beta in(0,1/4],

    lim_m B_(n_m) E_m(1/4,beta)
       =Z_1 exp(-1/4)/(2 lambda C_beta^4)<7/10.       (1.5)

The same argument controls the explicitly specified nonfixed scaling
beta_m=8/m. More precisely,

    lim_m sup_(8/m<=beta<=1/4) B_(n_m) E_m(1/4,beta)
       =Z_1 exp(-1/4)/(2 lambda)<7/10.               (1.6)

Thus the supremum in(1.6) is below7/10 for every sufficiently large m.
Over the full tau rectangle the corresponding limit of the supremum is
Z_1 exp(-1/5)/(2lambda)<3/4, also giving an eventual uniform deficit.

These are upper bounds on this ONE unchanged three-coordinate witness
family. They are NOT upper bounds on W_n, and do not settle the original
all-n/all-nonnegative-word minimax question. They do not control beta<8/m
(for example beta=1/m), other checkpoint selections or counts, or other
static functions. The smaller-beta region remains open in this report.
No positive pre-tail limit has been added to the terminal payoff.

## 2. The actual circular body and finite local construction

Fix beta in(0,1/4]. Define

    R_circ=1+beta, C_beta=beta+sqrt(1+2beta),
    p(q)=beta+sqrt(R_circ^2-(q+beta)^2), 0<=q<=1,
    v(q)=(p(q),-q),
    K_ctr=conv({0} union {v(q):0<=q<=1}).           (2.1)

The circular center is(beta,beta); its disk contains0 because
2beta^2<=(1+beta)^2. Thus its radial normals support this convex hull
on the displayed arc. The endpoints are(C_beta,0) and(beta,-1).
For every(p,-q) in K_ctr,

    0<=p<=C_beta, 0<=q<=1, p>=beta*q,
    (p,0) belongs to K_ctr.                         (2.2)

For any compact convex K containing0 put

    E_K(r)=max_(g in K){<g,r>-||g||^2/2}.             (2.3)

Completing the square identifies its unique maximizer as the Euclidean
projection Pi_K(r). Its characterization is

    <r-Pi_K(r),g-Pi_K(r)> <=0 for every g in K.       (2.4)

Adding this inequality at two points shows
||Pi_K(r)-Pi_K(s)||^2<=<Pi_K(r)-Pi_K(s),r-s>.
The projection is therefore1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing
expressions in(2.3) gives the derivative Pi_K(r) and a remainder bounded
by ||s-r||^2. Hence E_K is convex, nonnegative, differentiable and1-smooth.
Its zero set is the polar cone of K: positive support in a direction
produces a positive value using a sufficiently small multiple of that
supporting vector, while nonpositive support makes0 the maximizer.
The cone of K_ctr is generated by its two nonzero endpoints, since

    (p,-q)=((p-beta*q)/C_beta)(C_beta,0)+q(beta,-1).

For sigma>0 it follows that

    E_(sigma K_ctr)(U,V)=0 iff U<=0 and beta*U<=V.   (2.5)

Use the one-sided Huber function

    Hub_delta(z)=0 for z<=0;
                 z^2/2 for 0<=z<=delta;
                 delta*z-delta^2/2 for z>=delta.    (2.6)

It is convex and1-smooth, with derivative min(delta,max(z,0)).

Here is a finite construction for the ACTUAL ordinary block H_J, with
its ordered steps h_1,...,h_N, N=2^J-1. Set alpha_i=h_i/2. Construct
r_i=(u_i,-y_i) backward from

    r_N=(beta,-1), q_N=1.

For each i from N down to1 choose the unique q_(i-1) in[0,1) satisfying

    (q_(i-1)+beta)/(p(q_(i-1))-beta)
       =max{ beta/(C_beta-beta),
               [y_i+(1-alpha_i)beta]
                    /[u_i-(1-alpha_i)beta] },      (2.7)

and set

    r_(i-1)=r_i+alpha_i v(q_(i-1)).                 (2.8)

All our physical steps are positive. The denominator in(2.7) is positive
because u_i>=beta and alpha_i>0. Its left side is continuous and strictly
increasing from beta/(C_beta-beta) to infinity. Thus every term is defined.
The symbols specify a finite mathematical recursion; it was not executed.

For clarity, the projection checks underlying this construction are as
follows. Inductively u_i>=beta and y_i>=1, and the updates increase both
when read backward. At an interior projected arc point the centered ray
has ratio(y_i+beta)/(u_i-beta). Subtracting alpha_i*beta from its numerator
and adding alpha_i*beta to its denominator can only decrease that ratio.
Thus(2.7) gives q_(i-1)<=q_i. At q_i=1 this upper bound is immediate. At
the capped endpoint q_i=0 the supporting condition is

    y_i<=beta(u_i-C_beta)/(C_beta-beta).             (2.9)

It makes the second entry in the maximum in(2.7) no greater than the first,
so q_(i-1)=0 as well. At an uncapped new point, (2.7)--(2.8) place r_(i-1)
on the outward radial ray of v(q_(i-1)): the cross-product condition is
exactly(2.7), and y_(i-1)>=1>=q_(i-1) gives a radial factor at least1.
The circular normal then proves(2.4). At the capped new point, the maximum
condition instead gives(2.9) for i-1. The displacement from(C_beta,0) is a
nonnegative combination of the circular endpoint normal and(0,1), which
also proves(2.4). The terminal r_N is on K_ctr. We have proved

    Pi_(K_ctr)(r_i)=v(q_i),
    u_i>=beta, 1<=y_i<=y_0, 0<=q_i<=1.             (2.10)

In particular, with u=u_0, y=y_0 and S=S_J,

    u=beta+sum_i alpha_i p(q_(i-1)),
    y=1+sum_i alpha_i q_(i-1),
    u>=beta*y, M=u+y/beta>0.                       (2.11)

The inequality is termwise p>=beta*q. For a general incoming threshold
ell>=0 and amplitude D>0 define

    sigma=D/M, ell_out=sigma*y,
    A=ell+ell_out/beta,
    Phi(X,Y)=E_(sigma K_ctr)(X-A,Y-ell_out).        (2.12)

This is one fixed finite smooth convex function, zero whenever X<=ell
and Y>=0 by(2.5). Also its OUTPUT derivative is zero whenever Y>=ell_out,
for every X. Indeed replacing a gradient(p,-q) by(p,0) increases the
maximized expression by q(Y-ell_out)+q^2/2 if q>0.

The physical ordinary states are

    (X_i,Y_i)=(A+sigma*u_i,ell_out-sigma*y_i).       (2.13)

They start at(ell+D,0), and(2.8)--(2.10) show that each physical step h_i
updates them by -(h_i/2)grad Phi. Each query has X_i>ell and
0<=Y_i<=ell_out-sigma<ell_out. The final local gradient is
sigma(beta,-1). A physical checkpoint b>2, with T=b/2-1, sends it to

    (X^+,Y^+)=(A-beta*sigma*T,ell_out+sigma*T).      (2.14)

This is an exact half-step identity for the original physical b. It is
in the zero set(2.5). Nothing here substitutes a shortened physical word.

## 3. One static global function, every step, and its radius

For our identical ordinary blocks H_J, use the SAME u,y,M in both.
Put

    T_1=b_J/2-1, T_2=b_(J+1)/2-1,
    sigma_1=1/M, D_2=T_1/M,
    sigma_2=D_2/M, D_3=T_1*T_2/M^2,
    ell_2=y/M, ell_3=D_2*y/M,
    A_1=ell_2/beta, A_2=ell_2+ell_3/beta,
    delta=D_3/(1+S_L).                            (3.1)

For sufficiently large m the two checkpoints exceed2; all the parameters
are then positive. Define, before starting GD,

    Phi_1(X,Y)=E_(sigma_1 K_ctr)(X-A_1,Y-ell_2),
    Phi_2(Y,Z)=E_(sigma_2 K_ctr)(Y-A_2,Z-ell_3),
    F(X,Y,Z)=(Phi_1(X,Y)+Phi_2(Y,Z)
                                      +Hub_delta(Z-ell_3))/2. (3.2)

The function is convex and1-smooth. To check the constant, the linear map
stacking(X,Y),(Y,Z),Z has norm sqrt(2), since its squared output norm is
X^2+2Y^2+2Z^2. The separable sum of the three1-smooth functions has gradient
Lipschitz constant1 on the product space. Composition and the factor1/2
therefore give at most(1/2)*2=1. Every summand is nonnegative and F(0)=0.

At any point write the two projections as(P_1,-Q_1),(P_2,-Q_2), and put
P_3=min(delta,max(Z-ell_3,0)). The exact full update is

    X^+=X-h P_1/2,
    Y^+=Y-h(P_2-Q_1)/2,
    Z^+=Z-h(P_3-Q_2)/2.                            (3.3)

All components in this formula belong to the SAME static function.
During the first H_J and b_J the first pair follows(2.13)--(2.14);
its output is at most ell_2 before the checkpoint. The next component
and final Huber are zero there, so they do not change these updates.
The first checkpoint gives

    X=A_1-beta*D_2, Y=ell_2+D_2, Z=0.

During the next H_J and its root checkpoint b_(J+1), the second pair
follows(2.13)--(2.14), with ell=ell_2,D=D_2. The first component stays
zero in the second ordinary block: its input is fixed at or below A_1,
and its output is at least ell_2. In particular Q_1=0 at every active
query. The final Huber is inactive while the third coordinate is below
ell_3. Therefore the actual state just after the root checkpoint is

    v_in=A_1-beta*D_2,
    w_in=A_2-beta*D_3,
    Z_in=ell_3+D_3.                                (3.4)

This derives the incoming data without importing an unverified trajectory.

The ENTIRE minimum set is the simultaneous zero set of the summands:

    X<=A_1, Y>=beta*X,
    Y<=A_2, Z>=beta*(Y-ell_2),
    Z<=ell_3.                                     (3.5)

Here(2.5) and beta*A_1=ell_2 were used. From(2.11),

    1-A_1=sigma_1*u, ell_2=sigma_1*y,
    0<A_1<=1/(1+beta^2).                           (3.6)

The point x_star=(A_1,ell_2,0) belongs to(3.5). For any point of(3.5),
if X<0 its squared distance from e_1 is at least1. For 0<=X<=A_1,
its squared distance is at least(1-X)^2+beta^2 X^2, because Y>=beta X.
This quadratic decreases until X=1/(1+beta^2), so it is minimized on
[0,A_1] at A_1. The displayed x_star attains this bound, which is at most1.
Thus the TRUE closest-minimizer squared radius is exactly

    R_true^2=(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2
            =sigma_1^2(u^2+y^2)>0.                 (3.7)

This uses every constraint in the actual minimum set. The radius has not
been normalized using a freely chosen more distant minimizer.

Every smooth-convex interpolation row follows from this actual function.
For completeness, subtract <grad F(y),z> from F(z). Its minimum is attained
at y. The1-smooth descent bound at x with displacement
-(grad F(x)-grad F(y)) then yields
F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y> >=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.
This holds for every ordered pair, including x_star and the terminal sample.
Actual Euclidean Gram matrices are positive semidefinite.
For general smoothness L_smooth>0, take f=L_smooth F and physical updates
x^+=x-(h/L_smooth)grad f; the same trajectory and normalized payoff result.

## 4. Exact tail dynamics and all terminal terms

Throughout the entire remaining H_L the third-coordinate excess is

    Z-ell_3=D_3-s'*delta/2>=delta,
                         0<=s'<=S_L,              (4.1)

where s' is the physical mass already traversed. This statement is an
exact trajectory identity, justified inductively: it keeps Q_2=0 by the
output-isolation property in Section2, so P_3=delta and the displayed
update continues. In particular the old second component has the exact
horizontal expression

    Phi_2(w,Z)=Hub_(C_beta*sigma_2)(w-A_2).          (4.2)

The first coordinate never increases by(3.3), and hence v<=A_1. For any
such v its projection lies on the sloped segment p=beta*q: for fixed q,
reducing p to beta*q increases the expression being maximized, since
v-A_1<=0 and p>=0. The segment is in K_ctr. Maximization along it gives

    Phi_1(v,w)=Hub_((1+beta^2)*sigma_1)(beta*v-w)/(1+beta^2). (4.3)

Put sigma=sigma_1, kappa_tail=1+beta^2, and cap_ratio=C_beta*D_2.
The exact dynamics for EVERY physical tail step h are therefore

    q=min(sigma,max(beta*v-w,0)/kappa_tail),
    r=min(cap_ratio*sigma,max(w-A_2,0)),
    v^+=v-h*beta*q/2,
    w^+=w+h*(q-r)/2.                               (4.4)

There is a closed flat interval beta*v<=w<=A_2, with actual width

    g=A_2-beta*v,
    g_in=ell_3/beta+beta^2*D_2>0,
    g^+=g+h*beta^2*q/2>=g.                         (4.5)

Thus q and r cannot be positive together. When the pair enters that
interval both vanish, the pair is stationary, and(4.1) keeps it decoupled
from the still evolving third coordinate. This is exact absorption.

At the final point the ENTIRE payoff is

    E_m=[Phi_1(v_fin,w_fin)+Phi_2(w_fin,Z_fin)
                           +D_3^2/(2(1+S_L))]/R_true^2. (4.6)

Indeed substituting(4.1) at s'=S_L into(2.6) gives the displayed last
term. Until absorption is proved, both old terms in(4.6) are retained.

## 5. A finite last-crossing absorption lemma

This is the comparison used in this round. It treats the true quadratic
caps, the moving interval and all large steps exactly. It is NOT a
continuous-time limit or an approximation by the total word mass.

Consider(4.4) on a finite recursive word H_L with positive step values
b_0,...,b_(L-1), for fixed sigma>0, cap_ratio>0, beta>0 and A_2. Suppose
its incoming state has w_in<A_2 and positive width g_in=A_2-beta*v_in.
Let

    P_j=sum_{h in H_j, h>=2} h,
    z_in=beta*v_in-w_in,
    kappa_tail=1+beta^2.                            (5.1)

Empty sums are0. Suppose the following FINITE inequalities hold:

    max(z_in,0) <= kappa_tail*sigma*P_L/2;          (5.2)
    sigma*(b_j-cap_ratio*P_j)/2 <= g_in,
                                     0<=j<L;       (5.3)
    sigma*(cap_ratio*b_j-kappa_tail*P_j)/2 <= g_in,
                                     0<=j<L.       (5.4)

Then the actual pair has entered its flat interval by the end of H_L.

Proof. If the incoming point is in the interval, absorption already holds.
Otherwise w_in<A_2 forces it to be on the left, with z_in>0. Assume for
contradiction that the terminal pair is active. Since a point in the closed
flat interval is absorbing, EVERY sample, including every post-step sample,
has then been strictly on the left or strictly on the right. There are
finitely many samples, so if a side change occurs it has a last occurrence.

While consecutive samples stay on the left, z=beta*v-w>0 evolves by

    z^+=z-h*kappa_tail*min(sigma,z/kappa_tail)/2.    (5.5)

If h>=2 and z<kappa_tail*sigma, the right side is(1-h/2)z<=0,
contradicting staying on that side. Consequently every such high step
has q=sigma and decreases z by h*kappa_tail*sigma/2. This also covers
the cap boundary; if it would land at0 that would be absorption or a
side change, which is excluded on a constant-side segment. Low steps
h<2 still cause nonnegative decrease. Likewise, on a constant right-side
segment, t=w-A_2>0 evolves by

    t^+=t-h*min(cap_ratio*sigma,t)/2,               (5.6)

so every h>=2 must have r=cap_ratio*sigma and decreases t by precisely
h*cap_ratio*sigma/2. This proves both necessary high-step mass losses
without neglecting any low step or near-boundary query.

If there is no side change, the whole H_L stays left, and(5.5) gives
z_in>kappa_tail*sigma*P_L/2, contrary to(5.2).

Otherwise let the last side change occur at a step of level j. Every
occurrence of b_j in H_L is immediately followed by a full H_j. This
follows by induction from H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j): a node's right subtree
is read immediately after the node, before any ancestor or later subtree.
That complete following H_j lies in the original remaining tail.

For a left-to-right change, at the preceding sample write z=beta*v-w>0
and g=A_2-beta*v>=g_in. The new right margin satisfies exactly

    t^+=h*q/2-z-g < b_j*sigma/2-g_in.              (5.7)

By lastness of the crossing the following H_j remains strictly right.
Equation(5.6) therefore requires t^+>cap_ratio*sigma*P_j/2.
Combined with(5.7) this contradicts(5.3).

For a right-to-left change, at the preceding sample put t=w-A_2>0.
The left coordinate v is unchanged on this step, and the new left margin
satisfies

    z^+=h*r/2-t-g < b_j*cap_ratio*sigma/2-g_in.     (5.8)

The following H_j remains strictly left, so(5.5) requires
z^+>kappa_tail*sigma*P_j/2, contradicting(5.4).
All cases contradict terminal activity. The flat interval is reached,
which proves the finite lemma.

This proof is valid even if the gap increases substantially and if many
earlier crossings occurred. It does not compare two different words of
equal mass. The exact following-subtree property is indispensable.

## 6. Uniform control of the local backward data

We now derive the bounds needed to verify ALL inequalities(5.2)--(5.4)
uniformly over growing m. No prefix-to-infinite-composition exchange occurs.
For fixed beta write

    I_beta=sqrt(2)(1+beta)-beta<2,
    Y_beta=beta(exp(I_beta/beta)-1),
    U_beta=beta+(C_beta-beta)(Y_beta+beta)/beta.     (6.1)

For the finite backward trajectory in Section2 put
w_i=(y_i+beta)/(q_i+beta). At each uncapped point the radial equation is
u_i-beta=(p(q_i)-beta)w_i. Taking a cross product of(2.8) with
v(q_(i-1)) cancels the alpha_i term and gives

    w_(i-1)=w_i[1+
       (p(q_(i-1))*q_i-q_(i-1)*p(q_i))
            /{beta*(p(q_(i-1))+q_(i-1))}].         (6.2)

At a transition to q_(i-1)=0 this same formula follows from y_(i-1)=y_i;
if both q values are0 it is equality. Thus all endpoint cases are covered.
The numerator is

    integral_(q_(i-1))^(q_i)
                 [p(q_(i-1))-q_(i-1)*p'(t)] dt.

After dividing by p(q_(i-1))+q_(i-1), the integrand is a weighted mean
of1 and -p'(t), hence is at most max(1,-p'(t)). Multiplying(6.2), using
1+x<=exp(x) and w_N=1, gives

    y+beta <= (q_0+beta)
       exp(beta^(-1) integral_(q_0)^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt)
       <= beta exp(beta^(-1) integral_0^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt). (6.3)

For the second inequality, log(1+q_0/beta)<=q_0/beta and the integrand
is at least1. The derivative is
p'(t)=-(t+beta)/(p(t)-beta). It crosses -1 at
(1+beta)/sqrt(2)-beta. Integrating on the two sides yields exactly I_beta.
In particular

    1<=y<=Y_beta<=beta*exp(2/beta).                 (6.4)

For any q_i>0, the radial relation and y_i<=Y_beta imply u_i<=U_beta.
The steps with q_(i-1)>0 form one contiguous final part of the ordinary
block. Their total sum of alpha_i p(q_(i-1)) is at most U_beta-beta,
by telescoping u_i. Since p>=beta and C_beta<2,

    u=beta+C_beta*S/2-E_gap,
    0<=E_gap<=C_beta*(U_beta-beta)/beta
                       <=4 beta^(-1) exp(2/beta).  (6.5)

All other steps have q_(i-1)=0 and p=C_beta, so they contribute no gap
in(6.5). Define the explicit bound

    K_bound(beta)=6 beta^(-1) exp(2/beta).          (6.6)

Combining M=u+y/beta with(6.4)--(6.5) proves

    |M-C_beta*S/2|<=K_bound(beta),
    |u-C_beta*S/2|<=K_bound(beta),
    y/beta<=exp(2/beta).                           (6.7)

These are estimates for arbitrary ordered finite ordinary blocks. They
have been derived jointly, rather than borrowed from a source exponent
or from the earlier unverified draft.

They suffice uniformly for the moving beta range in Section1. Since
beta^(-1)exp(2/beta) decreases with beta, beta>=8/m implies

    K_bound(beta)<=(3m/4)exp(m/4)<=m(4/3)^m.        (6.8)

The last estimate follows from exp(1/4)<sum_(r>=0)(1/4)^r=4/3.
It is a symbolic exponential bound, not a numerical evaluation.

For the actual word put v_mass=a_m/(c_m-2). Since
c_m(c_m-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>eta_m, one has0<v_mass<1. The finite mass
identity, proved by substitution into S_(j+1)=2S_j+b_j and S_0=0, is

    1+S_j=v_mass*c_m^j+(1-v_mass)*2^j.              (6.9)

As m grows, v_mass->1 and c_m->lambda uniformly for tau in[1/5,1/4].
The identity m log(1+tau/m)->tau is uniform there; for example integrating
1/(1+x) gives 0<=tau-m log(1+tau/m)<=tau^2/(2m).
Consequently, for J=m-2 and L=m-1,

    S_J/lambda^J -> exp(tau),
    S_L/lambda^L -> exp(tau)                       (6.10)

uniformly in tau. In particular S is bounded below by a positive constant
times lambda^m for all sufficiently large m. Because4/3<2<lambda,
(6.8)--(6.10) imply, for EVERY fixed nonnegative integer r,

    m^r(1+2^J+K_bound(beta))/S ->0                 (6.11)

uniformly on the rectangle. Polynomial factors times a fixed geometric
ratio below1 tend to0; this follows, for instance, by taking the ratio of
successive terms and bounding it eventually by a constant below1.
Equation(6.11) is the full uniform error estimate used below.

## 7. Every crossing level satisfies the finite comparison

Write d_m=c_m-2. Formula(6.9) gives the exact identity

    b_j-d_m*S_j=c_m-1-d_m*(1-v_mass)*2^j
                 <=c_m-1.                         (7.1)

The sum P_j in(5.1) obeys

    0<=S_j-P_j<=2(2^j-1)<=2^(j+1),                (7.2)

because each discarded step is below2 and the word has2^j-1 entries.
This is an inequality used within the last-crossing proof, not a claim
that the actual tail depends only on its mass.

Set epsilon_M=M-C_beta*S/2, so |epsilon_M|<=K_bound(beta). From the
EXACT cap_ratio=C_beta*(b_J-2)/(2M) and(7.1),

    cap_ratio-d_m
      ={ C_beta[(c_m-3)-d_m*(1-v_mass)*2^J]
                              -2d_m*epsilon_M }/(2M).       (7.3)

For all sufficiently large m, 2<c_m<3, 0<d_m<1 and1<C_beta<2. Therefore

    |cap_ratio-d_m|<=Xi_m,
    Xi_m=(1+2^J+K_bound(beta))/M.                   (7.4)

By(6.7) and(6.11), M>=S/4 eventually, uniformly. Hence S/M<=4 and
m^r Xi_m->0 for every fixed r. In particular cap_ratio->d uniformly,
and cap_ratio*d_m<kappa_tail eventually, since d^2<1<=kappa_tail.
We may also use cap_ratio<=2 and kappa_tail<=17/16<2 uniformly.

For EVERY j with0<=j<=J=L-1, (7.1)--(7.4) imply

    sigma*(b_j-cap_ratio*P_j)/2
      <=[|d_m-cap_ratio| S+c_m-1+2cap_ratio*2^J]/(2M)
      <=4 Xi_m.                                   (7.5)

Indeed the first term is at most2Xi_m using S/M<=4, and the other
terms are at most(1+2*2^J)/M<=2Xi_m. Likewise, using
cap_ratio*d_m-kappa_tail<0,

    sigma*(cap_ratio*b_j-kappa_tail*P_j)/2
      <=[cap_ratio(c_m-1)+2kappa_tail*2^J]/(2M)
      <=2 Xi_m.                                   (7.6)

These estimates cover all levels simultaneously, including small levels
whose steps could never cross the positive-width interval in the first
place. No large-bridge transition is left to a pointwise limiting map.

We now compare them to the ACTUAL incoming gap. Equations(6.7),(6.9)
and(6.11) give the following uniform asymptotics, where the difference
from each displayed beta-dependent expression tends to0:

    D_2 - d/C_beta ->0,
    D_3 - d/C_beta^2 ->0,
    A_1 ->0, ell_2 ->0,
    R_true^2 ->1.                                  (7.7)

For D_3, use b_(J+1)=c_m*b_J-(c_m-1), so T_2/S->1/2 uniformly, while
T_1/S->d/2 and M/S-C_beta/2->0. For A_1 use the stronger bound
y/(beta*M)<=exp(2/beta)/M from(6.7)--(6.8); this tends to0 uniformly.
The radius convergence follows from its EXACT identity(3.7).
Since C_beta<2, eventually D_2>=d/4 uniformly. Consequently(4.5) gives

    g_in>=beta^2 D_2>=16d/m^2.                     (7.8)

Together with m^2 Xi_m->0, (7.5)--(7.8) establish(5.3)--(5.4) uniformly
for every level of every sufficiently large word.

It remains to check the incoming condition(5.2). The incoming right
margin is w_in-A_2=-beta D_3<0. The incoming left margin is exactly

    z_in=beta D_3-beta^2 D_2-ell_3/beta
              <=beta D_3.                        (7.9)

By(7.7), D_3<=1 eventually, so max(z_in,0)<=1/4 uniformly. Meanwhile
(6.10),(7.2) and(6.7) yield

    kappa_tail*sigma*P_L/2
          - kappa_tail*lambda/C_beta ->0           (7.10)

uniformly. The second expression is larger than lambda/2>1 for all our
beta. The left side of(5.2)'s bound is thus at most1/4 while its right
side exceeds1 for all sufficiently large m. This proves(5.2).
If the incoming point were already flat, no comparison would be needed;
otherwise all hypotheses of the finite last-crossing lemma now hold.
The two retained components therefore vanish at the ACTUAL final state.
This proves(1.3), with every physical step accounted for.

The estimates explicitly pay the width scale of order m^(-2), the full
exponentially growing word, the small-step mass omitted only from a lower
bound, every possible last-crossing level, and all quadratic cap queries.
There is no unproved interchange of a fixed-prefix limit with a growing
composition. No comparison repair or change of the function was used.

## 8. Original benchmark normalization and the genuine deficit

Retain the original all-split definitions

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=r<n) Omega(C_r,C_(n-r-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=r<n) G(C_r,B_(n-r-1)),
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.                                  (8.1)

No denominator is recomputed or replaced. Accepted fact04219554e41d9750
states that Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m) B_(2^m-1) exists and is positive and
finite. Its enclosure at b=1,j=0 gives

    Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2=(203/100)^2<21/5,      (8.2)

where B_1=G(1,1)=4 follows directly from(8.1). This is the only accepted
input used beyond elementary statements proved jointly in this report.

From(6.10), uniformly in tau,

    B_(n_m)/(1+S_L) -> Z_1*lambda*exp(-tau).        (8.3)

Substitute(7.7), the EXACT zero retained values, and R_true^2->1 into
(1.3). Since d=1/lambda, (8.3) proves exactly the uniform approximation
(1.4). For a fixed beta>0 the moving rectangle includes it once m>=8/beta;
this proves(1.5). For beta_m=8/m, C_beta tends to1. The function C_beta
is strictly increasing in beta, so the uniform approximation also proves
the supremum limits in(1.6) and over the full tau rectangle. This uses
uniform control, not an exchange of an uncontrolled supremum and limit.

For explicit benchmark margins, lambda>12/5 because sqrt(2)>7/5, and
exp(1/4)>1+1/4 gives exp(-1/4)<4/5. Thus

    Z_1*exp(-1/4)/(2lambda)
          < (21/5)(4/5)/(24/5)=7/10.              (8.4)

Similarly exp(-1/5)<5/6 gives

    Z_1*exp(-1/5)/(2lambda)
          < (21/5)(5/6)/(24/5)=35/48<3/4.        (8.5)

The strict limiting margins and uniform convergence imply the stated
eventual finite-horizon deficits. These are symbolic inequalities, with
no saved benchmark arithmetic replay, numerical evaluation or new DP.

There is no conflict with the positive post-root retained value in the
source candidate. For fixed beta, (3.4) and(7.7) give
z_in->beta*d*(1-beta*C_beta)/C_beta^2>0. The finite tail proved above
erases that value exactly. The equal-mass tail counterexample from the
source candidate also remains intact: the present argument uses the
specific recursively nested following blocks, not a mass-only law.

## 9. Source provenance, residual scope and resource handoff

The unchanged physical construction originated in the direct convex
reconstruction of the shifted circular mechanism in Kaizhao Liu and
Yuhan Ye, Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for Gradient Descent: The Strongly
Convex Case, arXiv:2609.26873v1, https://arxiv.org/html/2609.26873v1 .

NOT import a strongly convex exponent theorem, a finite-condition-number
trajectory, a source relative-error normalization, or an extra checkpoint.
There are no external theorems used as black-box premises.

# A positive-height planar law on all three exact physical survivors

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max4; assigned analytical
invocation72, baseline ended71, ended ceiling72 / attempted ceiling73,
controller cap1. Dispatch master_guidance a32faa619edd28dc; consult OFF.

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL PROOF DRAFT, **UNVERIFIED**. Every new
calculation and construction below is part of this one joint draft. No
new fact is asserted, no formal submission is allocated or made, and no
unverified intermediate finding is used as a correctness premise. The
original arbitrary-word exact-profile target remains OPEN.

The actual nonlinear law below has positive terminal payoff above
10/(97 lambda^m) on EACH exact F/M/E word, for EVERY m>=16. Its separate,
correctly oriented ORIGINAL benchmark comparison is B_(N_X) V_X>21/20.
Thus the draft supplies physical counterexamples both to that proposed
upper level and to any improving-benchmark upper for these particular
three word families. It gives no conclusion about arbitrary schedules.

There is one geometric pattern, two distinct static functions: F has a
different slope scale, while M and E share precisely the same function.
All parameters are fixed from the complete known word before the run.
No original step, query, cap, contraction or final unit step is omitted.

## 1. Exact words, physical lists and elementary parameter bounds

All spaces are real Euclidean spaces, with the usual inner product and
norm; e1=(1,0), e2=(0,1) is the standard orthonormal basis of R2.
conv denotes convex hull. The normalized smoothness is1.

Put lambda=1+sqrt2, fix an integer m>=16, and set

    A=lambda^m,
    q_0=(), q_(j+1)=(q_j,1+(lambda-2)lambda^j,q_j),
    zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A))/2,
    e=A/zeta=(zeta-1)/2,
    p_m=(q_m,1+e).

Here q_j and p_m are physical step lists, not gradient vectors.
These are the exact BASIC lambda words in TASK.md, not any enclosing-m
tau=1/4 hierarchy. Every child uses the fixed lambda above.

Their exact counts and masses follow by induction:

    length(q_j)=2^j-1, sum q_j=lambda^j-1,
    length(p_m)=2^m, sum p_m=A+e.

Indeed the next sum is 2(lambda^j-1)+1+(lambda-2)lambda^j
=lambda^(j+1)-1. Every entry is positive. The cap is the physical
step1+e, and zeta^2-zeta=2A gives 2e^2+e=A, so0<e<sqrt A.

For clarity give the exterior constants separate names:

    a=7/10, b=6/5, k=3/5, tau=3/5=k,
    b1=1+aA, b2=1+bA.

The complete physical words are

    H_F=(p_m,b1,q_m,tau,b2,q_m,1),
    H_M=(q_m,b1,p_m,tau,b2,q_m,1),
    H_E=(q_m,b1,q_m,tau,b2,p_m).

Write each as (L0,b1,L1,tau,b2,L2), with these COMPLETE lists:

| Word X | L0 | S0=sum L0 | L1 | S1=sum L1 | L2 | S2=sum L2 |
|---|---|---:|---|---:|---|---:|
| F | p_m | A+e | q_m | A-1 | (q_m,1) | A |
| M | q_m | A-1 | p_m | A+e | (q_m,1) | A |
| E | q_m | A-1 | q_m | A-1 | p_m | A+e |

Thus N_F=N_M=3*2^m+2 and N_E=3*2^m+1, the ACTUAL horizons.
For F/M the final unit step is explicitly inside L2. For E the objective
cap is explicitly inside L2. The front/middle objective cap in F/M is
inside the indicated first/middle list. All internal q_m queries remain.

The following small bound will pay every endpoint guard:

    (e+8/5)/A < 1/200.                               (1)

Since lambda>2 and m>=16, A>65536, e/A<1/256, and
8/(5A)<1/1000. Finally1/256+1/1000<1/200 because1256<1280.
This proves(1) without a numerical trajectory or recurrence evaluation.

## 2. One static planar envelope, with every slope and weight specified

Define

    v=(-3/8,sqrt7/8), ||v||^2=1/4,
    t=t_X=A/(1+S0),
    P=t*e1/A, Q=t*v/A, R=-k*Q,
    D=conv{0,P,Q,R},
    f_X(x)=max_(g in D){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}, x in R2,
    x0=e1.                                          (2)

The local vector R in(2) is the last slope; it is distinct from the
project's scalar worst-case notation R_n=W_n/2.

In particular t_F=A/(A+e+1), t_M=t_E=1. M and E use the SAME
static function(2); their different words yield different actual ends.
There is no function switching, new origin, rescaling of a module's
starting point, new metric or oracle-dependent choice.

For an explicit affine-plane specification, the support function is

    J(u)=max{0,<P,u>,<Q,u>,<R,u>}.

Its four plane offsets are0 and its four weights are1. The vectors P,Q,R
are all printed in(2). The function in(2) is its unit quadratic envelope;
the definition by the attained maximum in(2) alone specifies it globally.
The positive endpoint heights below arise from the actual nonzero support
values, not unprinted intercepts or an added constant.

The radicand7 is positive. P and Q are linearly independent because
P has zero second coordinate and Q has positive second coordinate.
Since R=-3Q/5, zero lies on the segment[Q,R]. The triangle with vertices
P,Q,R has nonempty interior in the stated REAL two-dimensional metric.
All parameters, including t, depend only on the known complete word.

## 3. Global convexity, C1 smoothness, complete minimum and TRUE radius

For every x the maximizer in(2) is unique: completing the square makes
it the Euclidean projection g=P_D(x). The normal characterization is

    x=u+g, g in D,
    <u,z-g><=0 for every z in D.                     (3)

To justify this directly, for any z in D the squared-distance difference
is ||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2=||z-g||^2-2<u,z-g>. Condition(3) gives
minimality; the necessary inequality follows by differentiating along
the segment from g to z at its initial endpoint. Strict convexity of
the squared distance gives uniqueness.

Equivalently g is a support maximizer for J(u). It is enough to check
the four vertices0,P,Q,R because every z in D is their convex combination.
For any such pair the exact value is

    f_X(x)=<u,g>+||g||^2/2.                          (4)

The normals for x,g and x',g', applied with each other's projected
vectors, give <x-x',g-g'>>=||g-g'||^2. Cauchy--Schwarz proves that
the projection map is1-Lipschitz. The two attained maxima bound

    0<=f_X(x+d)-f_X(x)-<g,d><=<g'-g,d><=||d||^2,
    g'=P_D(x+d).

Thus f_X is differentiable, grad f_X=P_D, and its gradient is globally
1-Lipschitz and continuous. The function is finite everywhere because
D is compact, and convex as a supremum of affine functions of x.
On the nonempty interior of D, projection is the identity, giving
f_X(x)=||x||^2/2 there. Hence its least global smoothness is EXACTLY1.
It is nonquadratic: on an open normal cone beyond P the gradient is the
constant P, whereas it is the identity on the triangle's interior.

Since0 is in D, f_X>=0. Its COMPLETE minimum set and minimum value are

    Cmin={x:<P,x><=0,<Q,x><=0,<R,x><=0}
        ={x:x1<=0, -3x1+sqrt7*x2=0},
    min f_X=0.                                     (5)

Sufficiency follows since every g in D then has <x,g><=0.
For necessity, if one vertex z has <x,z>>0, a sufficiently small
positive multiple eps*z belongs to D and makes
eps<x,z>-eps^2||z||^2/2>0 in(2). Opposite Q/R force equality in
their common direction. In particular0 is an attained minimizer.

For any x in Cmin, ||e1-x||^2=(1-x1)^2+x2^2>=1, with equality only
at0. Thus the complete minimum set's UNIQUE closest point to x0 is0,
and the TRUE initial radius is1. This is independent of X and m.

## 4. Every initialized first-list query and the first bridge

At each original query in L0, and at its FOLLOWING b1 query, let c be
the exact sum of the original entries of L0 already taken. Thus0<=c<=S0.
Propose

    x(c)=e1-cP,
    g(c)=P,
    u(c)=x(c)-P=[1-(c+1)/(S0+1)]e1,
    f_X(x(c))=1/(S0+1)-(c+1/2)/(S0+1)^2.            (6)

The coefficient of e1 in u(c) is nonnegative. All support comparisons
in(3) are exhaustive:

    H_P=<P,u(c)>, H_P>=0,
    <Q,u(c)>=(-3/8)*H_P<=0,
    <R,u(c)>=(9/40)*H_P<=H_P,
    zero height=0<=H_P.

Here H_P names only this displayed first-sector support height.
At c=S0 all support heights
are0, but the unique projection at x=P is still P. Formula(3) proves
every proposed first-list jet of(6) for the SAME static function.

For any next physical L0 entry h_i, literal subtraction gives
x(c)-h_i P=e1-(c+h_i)P, the next original query. This proves ALL
initialized updates by induction, including every internal q_m step
and the objective cap when X=F. No module is replaced by a single oracle.

At the physical b1 query, x=P and g=P. Its ACTUAL update is

    P-(1+aA)P=-a*t*e1=:XQ.                         (7)

This retains the whole initialized prefix and produces the actual start
of the second list, rather than an independently normalized start.

## 5. Every middle-list query, its objective cap, the contraction and b2

At each physical query in L1, the query of tau, and the query of b2,
let s be the exact mass already taken in the complete list(L1,tau).
It lies in0<=s<=S1+tau. Propose the following ACTUAL jets:

    x(s)=XQ-sQ,
    g(s)=Q,
    u(s)=XQ-(s+1)Q,
    f_X(x(s))=<Q,u(s)>+||Q||^2/2.                  (8)

Write z=(s+1)/A. Since S1 is A-1 or A+e, (1) gives

    0<z<=1+1/200.

All four support heights in(3) are

    <Q,u(s)>=(t^2/A)[21/80-z/4],
    <P,u(s)>=(t^2/A)[-7/10+3z/8],
    <R,u(s)>=-k<Q,u(s)>,
    zero height=0.                                (9)

The Q height is strictly greater than or equal to
(t^2/A)[1/80-1/800]=(t^2/A)*9/800>0, since z<=1+1/200.
The P height is at most(t^2/A)[-7/10+(3/8)(201/200)]<0;
the last sign follows from603<1120 after multiplication by1600.
The R height is negative. Thus Q uniquely wins every support comparison.
Condition(3) proves(8) for EVERY original middle query, including the
cap1+e when X=M, the tau query and the b2 query.

For each actual L1 or tau step h_i, x(s)-h_i Q=XQ-(s+h_i)Q.
This proves EVERY one of these initialized updates. In particular the
tau=3/5 step leaves the gradient Q unchanged; no quadratic damping
relation is imposed. It subtracts tau*Q from the position and subtracts
tau*||Q||^2 from its positive support height, which remains strictly
positive by(9).

At the end of a q_m middle list, before tau, the height is exactly
t^2/(80A); when the middle list is p_m it is
(t^2/A)[1/80-(e+1)/(4A)]. At the b2 query the heights are respectively

    F/E: (t^2/A)[1/80-3/(20A)],
    M:   (1/A)[1/80-(e+8/5)/(4A)].                  (10)

These are PAID POSITIVE endpoint heights, of order1/A, rather than
zero-height endpoints. They are the mechanism that keeps the actual
subunit contraction on the affine face.

The actual b2 update, still using Q, is

    XR=XQ-(S1+tau+b2)Q.                            (11)

This is the initialized start of the entire last physical list.

## 6. Every last-list query, final unit or objective cap, and terminal

At each physical last-list query and at the ACTUAL unqueried terminal,
let w be the exact mass already taken in L2, so0<=w<=S2. Define

    C(w)=(S1+tau+b2-k(w+1))/A
        =C0-k*w/A,
    C0=b+(S1+1)/A,
    x(w)=XR-wR,
    g(w)=R,
    u(w)=x(w)-R=t[-a*e1-C(w)*v].                  (12)

The equality for C0 uses the literal tau=k cancellation and retains
both the1 in b2 and the1 in the proximal subtraction. The three starts
and ends are

| X | C0 | C_N=C(S2) |
|---|---:|---:|
| F | 11/5 | 8/5 |
| M | 11/5+(e+1)/A | 8/5+(e+1)/A |
| E | 11/5 | 8/5-3e/(5A) |

By(1), all actual C(w), including both endpoints, satisfy

    3/2<C(w)<11/5+1/200.                           (13)

For the lower bound the only decreasing endpoint is E, and
8/5-3/(1000)>3/2. For the upper bound use(e+1)/A<1/200.
The strict upper statement also holds in the other cases.

All four original support comparisons are explicitly

    <Q,u(w)>=(t^2/A)[21/80-C(w)/4]<0,
    <R,u(w)>=(t^2/A)[3C(w)/20-63/400]
                            >(t^2/A)*27/400>0,
    <R-P,u(w)>=(t^2/A)[217/400-9C(w)/40]
                            >(t^2/A)*371/8000>0,
    zero height=0.                                (14)

The first two follow from C(w)>3/2. The third uses the upper bound:
217/400-(9/40)(11/5+1/200)=19/400-9/8000=371/8000.
Thus R wins against P, Q and0 throughout the COMPLETE last list and
at the actual terminal. Condition(3) proves g(w)=R. The exact value is

    f_X(x(w))=(t^2/A)[3C(w)/20-63/400]
                                   +9t^2/(200A^2). (15)

For EVERY remaining physical step h_i, x(w)-h_i R=XR-(w+h_i)R,
and the next partial mass stays in[0,S2]. This proves the full actual
suffix by induction. It includes every q_m internal query, the final
unit step for F/M, and the terminal objective cap1+e for E. The terminal
gradient in(12) is derived from the SAME globally specified law, even
though the GD updates do not query it.

No queried minimum appears in this suffix: (14) gives a strictly
positive support height at every listed point. This law was changed
BEFORE initialization; it is not a completion of the absorbing prefix
in eef58699050dac8a or c2e2afdfda4974a4.

## 7. Exact physical indexing: no endpoint-only oracle shorthand

Let ell0=length(L0) and ell1=length(L1). Original indices are zero-based.
The first gradient sector contains every L0 query0,...,ell0-1 and the
b1 queryell0. The next sector starts at jQ=ell0+1, contains all L1
queriesjQ,...,jQ+ell1-1, the tau queryjQ+ell1, and the b2 query
jQ+ell1+1. The last sector starts at jR=ell0+ell1+3 and contains every
original L2 queryjR,...,N_X-1 and the additional terminal pointN_X.

For each first-sector original index i, the c in(6) is
sum_(0<=u<i)h_u. For each second-sector index i, the s in(8) is
sum_(jQ<=u<i)h_u. For each last-sector index i, the w in(12) is
sum_(jR<=u<i)h_u. These are the original chronological sums over
ALL entries, including the caps, contraction and unit step where present.
Every individual update is proved in Sections4--6 using its literal h_i.

The endpoint-only list decomposition is a notation for these complete
query sectors, not an invocation of a module as an oracle. No radius
transport between modules or deletion of internal gradients is used.

## 8. Every original ordered interpolation row, remote rows and metric

For every reached sample i, including the terminal, set

    g_i=grad f_X(x_i), u_i=x_i-g_i,
    f_i=f_X(x_i)=<u_i,g_i>+||g_i||^2/2.

Add any minimizer x_star in the complete set(5), g_star=0 and f_star=0;
then u_star=x_star. For all these samples, the normal inequality(3)
holds with EVERY z in D. For the minimizer it follows directly from(5).
For any ordered pair i,j, direct cancellation gives the ENTIRE original
signed residual from accepted fact_id1252b8823bb5a290:

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<u_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                         (16)

The inequality is the normal at j with allowed comparison vector g_i.
It therefore covers BOTH orientations, every old/future/remote pair,
every cap/contraction/unit query, the unqueried terminal and ANY minimum.
There is no adjacency-only or formal endpoint feasibility test.
The full Gram of(x0,g0,...,g_(N_X)) is a Gram of actual vectors in
the displayed REAL Euclidean R2, so it is positive semidefinite.

## 9. Exact unqueried terminal and W=2R payoff

By(12), with the printed C_N in its table,

    x_(N_X)=t[-a*e1-(C_N+k/A)v],
    grad f_X(x_(N_X))=R=-k*t*v/A,
    u_(N_X)=t[-a*e1-C_N*v].                         (17)

The minimum is0 and the TRUE initial radius is1 by(5). Thus the actual
normalized payoff, in the project's W=2R normalization, is

    V_X=2f_X(x_(N_X))
       =(t^2/A)[3C_N/10-63/200]+9t^2/(100A^2).

Substitution of the three exact C_N values gives

    V_F=[A/(A+e+1)]^2*[33/(200A)+9/(100A^2)],
    V_M=33/(200A)+[3e/10+39/100]/A^2,
    V_E=33/(200A)-9e/(50A^2)+9/(100A^2).             (18)

All quantities in(17)--(18) are from the complete initialized run of
the SINGLE static function(2), with the actual physical horizonN_X.
In particular no intermediate point is reported as the terminal.

Each of these satisfies, uniformly for EVERY m>=16,

    V_X>3/(20A)>10/(97A).                           (19)

For M the positive correction makes A V_M>33/200>3/20.
For E, (1) gives A V_E>33/200-9/10000=1641/10000>1500/10000.
For F, t>200/201 and t^2>10/11 since440000>404010. Hence
A V_F>(33/200)*(10/11)=3/20. Finally3/20>10/97 follows from291>200.
This rejects the proposed q_goal=10/(97A) all-function upper for each

In fact their common leading physical payoff is

    lim_(m->infinity) A V_X=33/200,                  (20)

since e/A<1/sqrt A tends to0, 1/A tends to0 and t_F tends to1.
This is a proved formula inside the joint UNVERIFIED draft, not a fact.

## 10. Separate ORIGINAL benchmark comparison in the correct direction

Keep the ORIGINAL ALL-INTEGER-SPLIT recurrences:

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

The unconditional benchmark portions of accepted fact_id04219554e41d9750
give monotonicity and B_(2n+1)>=lambda B_n (proof Sections3 and7,

benchmark upper from1ef7c860ceeda980, phase optimizer or executed DP is

For an explicit small initial comparison, B_1=G(1,1)=4. The admissible

    B_2>=G(1,4)=4+2sqrt3>7.

The strict last inequality follows from3>9/4, so sqrt3>3/2.
Iterating the ACCEPTED doubling lower bound along n_j=3*2^j-1 gives

    B_(3*2^m-1)>=lambda^m B_2>7A.

Both ACTUAL horizons in Section1 exceed3*2^m-1. Monotonicity therefore
gives B_(N_X)>7A. Combining with(19) proves the correctly oriented lower
comparison

    B_(N_X) V_X>21/20,
    W_(N_X)(H_X)>=V_X>(21/20) U_(N_X)^ZJ,
    X in{F,M,E}, EVERY m>=16.                       (21)

Thus this witness does more than reject q_goal: these EXACT three word
families cannot provide any improvement over the original ZJ benchmark.
This statement is only about those physical families. It does NOT rule
out some other word, and it does NOT establish the original uniform
all-word matching lower bound.

For arbitrary advertised L>0 use the single objective L*f_X and the
prescribed physical steps h_i/L. Every initialized point in Sections4--9
is unchanged, values and gradients are multiplied by L, smoothness is
sharply L, the complete minimum and TRUE radius remain(5), and payoff
2[L f_X(x_N)-min(L f_X)]/[L*1^2] is exactly V_X. Every original row
with squared-gradient denominator2L is L times(16), hence nonpositive.

## 11. Dependency, retrieval and adversarial audit discipline

FULL required shared findings1ef7c860ceeda980, eef58699050dac8a and

Work in progress, UNVERIFIED. For original H_m set r=2/c_m, Z_m=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)r^j, w_j=r^j/Z_m. On the full binary tree take one orthonormal coordinate per internal node and each leaf feature a_s equal to the signed root-to-leaf sum of sqrt(w_j) times those coordinates. F_m=log(2^(-m)sum_s exp(<a_s,x>)) has unique minimum0 and sharp global gradient Lipschitz constant Ltree=(1+w_0)/2. Start only on the leftmost spine with sqrt(w_j)*x_(0^j)=log2. Exact R^2=(log2)^2*r^(1-m)*Z_m^2. GD with h_i/Ltree stays in this m-dimensional spine but remains nonlinearly coupled. Its initial exit-group masses are 1/(m+4) for each of m side groups and4/(m+4) at the last leaf. Exact update y_(i+1),j=y_i,j-(h_i*w_j/Ltree)(sum_(k>j)P_i,k-P_i,j). The needed diagnostic B_n*V>=1 is EXACTLY final entropy >=Ltree*R^2/(2B_n), of order2^(-m); this estimate is not proved. Child gradients are multiplied by the changing current subtree mass, so a fresh original-word child recurrence is not justified. Complete joint evidence and all scope guards will follow within the same round.

# C105: charged obtuse endpoints and a whole original-word obstruction

Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT, allocation round36 of ceiling37.
This is not a fact. It closes the declared obtuse family at draft level,
including every original checkpoint pair, but not the original target.
The current assignment and guidance48a4276698805f28 permit textual work
and shared drafts only. No computation or formal verification is used.

Original target: with the original last-iterate, true nearest-minimizer
normalization W=2R, prove W_n^*/U_n^ZJ ->1 uniformly over all sufficiently
large lengths and all predetermined nonnegative words, or certify a fixed
relative asymptotic full-class upper improvement. All finite dimensions,
zero and unbounded steps, and one static function per run remain allowed.
Nothing below is an upper bound on the unrestricted W_n.

The accepted inputs are1252b8823bb5a290 (row convention and normalization),
b98bb807d514e80d (complete K and original benchmark definitions),
1fca3e5dd555cb4a (complete ray channel), and dfde2fa326c908bf (complex/lens
channel). C101--C104 are retrieval evidence, not correctness predecessors.
All new ingredients used below are derived within this joint document.

MAIN CONCLUSION. For the entire legal family (1)--(6), on every original
H_m checkpoint pair with enclosing-m parameters unchanged, let V_m^obt
be the supremum of the exact true-distance payoff. Then

    limsup_(m->infinity) V_m^obt/K_(2^m-1)(H_m)<=13/10,
    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) V_m^obt<=13/[5 exp(1)]<1. (0)

Here the COMPLETE same-word K equals COMPLETE F_ray and is dominated
by both full Huber-augmented complex/lens references. Section6 gives
the stronger finite factor133613/134064<1 for all parameters in one
structural pair class. Appendix C prices ALL remaining pairs, including
positive eta and small E, and proves the uniform asymptotic statement.
An obtuse instance might improve a reference by less than the bound;
we do not claim V_m^obt<=K. The whole family nevertheless misses the
original profile by a fixed relative asymptotic amount. This is an
adversary-family obstruction, not an upper bound on unrestricted W_n.

## 1. Exact declared family and every physical support test

Fix the ORIGINAL word h=(block_0,b,block_1,d,block_2). Put

    S_j=sum block_j, T=1+S_0, M=1+S_1, N=1+S_2,
    A=b-1>0, E=d-1>0, sigma=2N-1.

Blocks contain their actual chronological physical steps, including zeros.
These letters are local abbreviations, not the original B_n benchmark.
Choose independent u>=0,v>0,alpha>=0,beta>=0,kappa>=0,w>0,ell>0. Set

    delta=A-Mu-alpha, H=Mv+beta,
    p=(1,0), q=(-u,-v), r=(-w,ell),
    a_q=u^2+v^2, a_r=w^2+ell^2, c=<q,r>=uw-v ell,
    z=(Eu-alpha,Ev-beta), eta=w alpha-ell beta,
    R_q=u alpha+v beta-kappa, G=R_q-eta.

Here eta is the r-plane height at the ending middle proximal point;
it is not a free reserve. Define one static finite affine maximum

    J(y)=max{0,<p,y>,<q,y>-kappa,<r,y>},
    F(x)=min_y {J(y)+||x-y||^2/2}, X_0=(T+delta,-H).       (1)

The complete endpoint tests for the stated constant gradients p,q,r are

    delta>=0, vH<=(1+u)delta+kappa, R_q>=0;              (2)
    G>=0, G+(M-1)(a_q-c)>=0;                            (3)
    <r,z>-N a_r>=0;                                    (4)
    <r-q,z>+kappa-(a_r-c)>=0,
    <r-q,z>+kappa-N(a_r-c)>=0;                          (5)
    <r-p,z>-N(a_r+w)>=0.                               (6)

Unlike the positive-quadrant case, (3) is not automatic, and the rate
a_r-c in (5) is not presumed positive. Both actual endpoints are kept.
The prefix r comparison is automatic: at its proximal point
y=(delta+S_0-t,-H), the p-minus-r margin is
(1+w)(delta+S_0-t)+ell H>0 for every physical t in[0,S_0].
The p-versus-zero and p-versus-q margins are minimized at t=S_0;
their values are exactly the first two conditions in (2).

Checkpoint b sends the iterate to(-Mu-alpha,-Mv-beta). At middle
cumulative mass t in[0,S_1] its proposed proximal point is

    y=(-alpha,-beta)+(S_1-t)q.

The q-versus-zero margin is R_q+(S_1-t)a_q>=0. Its margin over p
is nonnegative because p's height is nonpositive. Its margin over r
is G+(S_1-t)(a_q-c), so (3) proves every physical middle query.
The second checkpoint sends the iterate to z. At tail cumulative
mass t in[0,S_2], put k=1+t, so y=z-k r with1<=k<=N. The r-minus-zero
margin has positive loss rate a_r; the r-minus-p margin has positive
loss rate a_r+w. Their final tests are (4),(6). The r-minus-q margin
has rate a_r-c of unrestricted sign, so (5) proves every physical tail
query. This includes the actual terminal point.

These tests are necessary as well as sufficient for the specified
gradients of (1). A subgradient of a finite affine maximum is a convex
combination of its active slopes: otherwise a separating direction
contradicts its directional derivative, the maximum active directional
slope. The slopes p,q,r are uniquely exposed by directions(1,0),(0,-1),
(0,1), respectively. Hence their occurrence as subgradients requires
their respective planes to be active. Evaluating activity at the stated
endpoints gives exactly (2)--(6).

## 2. Static regularity and the actual nearest minimizer

J is continuous, finite, convex and nonnegative. Its proximal objective
is coercive and strictly convex, hence has a unique minimizer y(x).
Comparison with y+t(v-y), convexity and t decreasing to0 show
g(x)=x-y(x) belongs to the subdifferential of J at y(x). Conversely,
that subgradient inequality and the quadratic expansion prove proximal
optimality. Adding the subgradient inequalities at two points gives
||g(x)-g(x')||^2<=<g(x)-g(x'),x-x'>. In particular g is1-Lipschitz.
Evaluating the proximal objective at the old minimizer gives the upper
first-order bound for F. Applying its subgradient inequality at the new
minimizer and completing the quadratic gives the lower bound. Thus

    0<=F(x')-F(x)-<g(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2/2.

This proves that F is convex, differentiable and1-smooth with gradient g.
Its minimum is0 and its minimum set is exactly

    K={x_1<=0, -w x_1+ell x_2<=0, -u x_1-v x_2<=kappa}. (7)

Indeed J=0 gives F=0, and an attained zero proximal objective forces
both nonnegative terms to vanish. The geometry in (7) has been rederived
for w>0: it lies below the line x_2=(w/ell)x_1<=0 and above
x_2=(-kappa-u x_1)/v>=-kappa/v. It is a triangle when kappa>0 and
the singleton0 when kappa=0. Its left vertex has
x_1=-kappa/(u+vw/ell); the denominator is positive even when u=0.

Since T+delta>0 and H>0, the closest point to X_0 is

    t_star=min(H,kappa/v), x_star=(0,-t_star),
    D=(H-kappa/v)_+,
    R^2=dist(X_0,K)^2=(T+delta)^2+D^2.                  (8)

Proof: every point of K has first coordinate<=0 and second coordinate
>=-kappa/v. These separate lower bounds on squared coordinate distances
are simultaneously attained at the displayed point, which satisfies all
three inequalities of (7). This includes both positive-part branches.
Here R denotes this local true distance, not the project's ratio in W=2R.

## 3. All directed rows, optimum slacks, payoff and holding price

Assign slopes0,p,q,r affine costs0,0,kappa,0, respectively. At any of
the physical samples, activity at y_i=x_i-g_i gives

    f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2-k_i,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<g_i-g_j,y_j>-k_i+k_j<=0.                     (9)

The last inequality is precisely j-plane activity against i's plane.
Thus every orientation, all remote physical pairs, and repeated/zero-step
queries satisfy the project convention of accepted1252b8823bb5a290.
Include x_star with slope, cost and value0. Its zero plane is active,
so (9) also proves both optimum orientations. No interpolation row is
deleted and no Gram-only realization is substituted for the function.

The ending-query affine reserves are delta,R_q,L_0, with

    L_0=<r,z>-N a_r,
    L_p=<r-p,z>-N(a_r+w),
    L_q=<r-q,z>+kappa-N(a_r-c).

The optimum slacks k_i-<g_i,x_star> are0,kappa-v t_star,ell t_star.
They are nonnegative and are distinct from the affine reserves.
The exact true-distance payoff is

    V=2F(X_n)/R^2=P/R^2,
    P=2<r,z>-sigma a_r
     =2eta-2E c-sigma a_r>0.                           (10)

Its positivity follows from (4): P=2L_0+a_r>=a_r>0.
Replacing F by L F for arbitrary L>0 keeps the same trajectory under
the original steps h_i/L and the same normalized payoff.

For an appended nonnegative list of total mass tau, the SAME function
holds gradient r at every appended physical query exactly while

    0<=tau<=min{L_0/a_r, L_p/(a_r+w),
               L_q/(a_r-c) if a_r>c, otherwise +infinity}. (11)

The three margins become L_0-tau a_r, L_p-tau(a_r+w),
L_q-tau(a_r-c). A nonpositive loss rate imposes no upper limit.
Exposedness of r supplies necessity. During this exact interval
V(tau)=V(0)-2tau a_r/R^2. No reserve by itself licenses continuation.

## 4. Positive residual is paid twice; a conditional complete comparison

Put eta_+=max(eta,0). From R_q>=0 and G>=0 one has

    kappa<=u alpha+v beta-eta_+.

Combining this with prefix condition (2) gives

    Mv^2+eta_+<=delta+u(A-Mu),
    D>=(Mv-u alpha/v+eta_+/v)_+.                        (12)

The first inequality charges the residual to prefix activity; the second
charges it to the true radius. Define only for an UPPER RELAXATION

    v_hat=sqrt(v^2+eta_+/M).

Then M v_hat^2<=delta+u(A-Mu), and

    R^2>=(T+delta)^2+(M v_hat-u alpha/v_hat)_+^2.        (13)

For (13), the positive-part numerator M v_hat^2-u alpha is the same
on both sides; its actual denominator v is no larger than v_hat.
This does not assert that changing v yields a legal new trajectory.

For T>=2A, the full-norm calculation proved in Appendix A gives

    (u^2+v_hat^2)/R^2<=C(T,A,M),
    C(T,A,M)=max{A^2/(M^2T^2),1/(T+A+M)^2}.             (14)

Completing the terminal squares, with their actual signs retained,

    P=E^2(u^2+v^2)/sigma+2eta
      -sigma(ell-Ev/sigma)^2
      -(sqrt(sigma)w+Eu/sqrt(sigma))^2.                 (15)

Therefore the ENTIRE legal obtuse parameter family on a word satisfying
T>=2A obeys

    V<=max{E^2/sigma,2M} C(T,A,M).                      (16)

More sharply, if eta<=0 OR E^2>=2M sigma, then

    V<= E^2 C(T,A,M)/sigma.                            (17)

In the second case,2eta_+<=E^2 eta_+/(M sigma); in the first case
the extra term is nonpositive. These are explicit domain restrictions,
not assertions about all obtuse endpoints. Appendix B proves directly
that on every ORIGINAL H_m pair,m>=128, the right side of (17) is at
most(61473/68840)K_n(H_m), with complete K=F_ray and the original-B
limsup at most2(61473/68840)/exp(1)<1. Thus (17) closes a substantial
declared subdomain, but (16) alone does not close its complement.

A second upper bound, valid for EVERY legal obtuse tuple when T>=2A,
is obtained by dropping the negative terms -2Euw,-2ell beta and
completing w and ell separately:

    V<= [A^2/T^2+E^2 C(T,A,M)]/sigma.                   (18)

Here alpha<=A and R>=T, and (14) with eta_+=0 follows from the weaker
conditions kappa<=u alpha+v beta. Again this is an upper relaxation.

## 5. Middle-to-terminal transport: the third price cannot be removed

Evaluate the retained q-plane margin at the actual terminal point:

    L_q=E(a_q-c)-N(a_r-c)-G>=0.

Together with G>=0 this gives the necessary inequality

    N a_r<=(E) a_q+(N-E)c.                             (19)

When e=E/N<=1, Cauchy--Schwarz implies ||r||<=||q||: setting
t=||r||/||q||>0 in (19) gives t^2<=(1-e)t+e, or
(t-1)(t+e)<=0. More specifically, direct coordinate factorization gives

    (w-u)(w+e u)+(ell+v)(ell-e v)<=0.                 (20)

If w>u, it follows that ell<e v and

    (w-u)(w+e u)<=(ell+v)(e v-ell)<=e v^2,             (21)

the last inequality using e<=1. These are necessary whole-function
conditions from BOTH directions of the physical q,r rows. Keeping only
the middle row permits false states and misses (19).

Here is a quantitative reserve bound using this transport. Suppose

    A/T<=rho, E/N<=rho, 0<rho<=1/2, eta>0.

Then every legal tuple satisfies

    2eta/R^2<=rho/(2M),
    2eta/R^2-sigma w^2/R^2
       <=max_(0<=x<=rho/(4M)){2x-sigma x^2/rho^2}.      (22)

Proof of the first inequality. If eta<=u alpha, then
2M eta<=2(Mu)alpha<=A^2/2, so2M eta/R^2<=rho^2/2<=rho/2.
If eta>u alpha, alpha>0. Write zeta=eta-u alpha>0 and set
b_0=zeta/v, x_0=Mv, B_0=Mu. From eta<=w alpha, one has
w-u>=zeta/alpha. By (21), b_0<=alpha sqrt(e)<=alpha sqrt(rho).
Formula (12) gives R^2>=(T+delta)^2+(x_0+b_0)^2>=T^2+(x_0+b_0)^2,
while2M eta=2alpha B_0+2x_0 b_0 and B_0<=A-alpha.
Let C_0=rho/2. Completing the square in x_0 proves

    2x_0 b_0-C_0(x_0+b_0)^2<=b_0^2(1/C_0-2).

Because1/C_0-2>=0, the right side is at most
alpha^2 rho(2/rho-2)=2(1-rho)alpha^2. Thus

    2M eta-C_0 R^2
       <=2A alpha-2rho alpha^2-C_0 T^2
       <=2(1-rho)A^2-(rho/2)T^2<=0.

The middle inequality uses alpha<=A and rho<=1/2, so the displayed
quadratic is increasing on[0,A]. The last follows from A<=rho T and
4rho(1-rho)<=1. This proves the first part of (22). For the second,
set x=eta/R^2. Since eta<=w alpha and alpha/R<=A/T<=rho,
w^2/R^2>=x^2/rho^2. The allowed interval for x is the first part.
The maximum is explicit:

    rho/(2M)-sigma/(16M^2), if sigma<=4rho M;
    rho^2/sigma,             if sigma>=4rho M.          (23)

Combining (15),(14),(23) yields an additional whole-family upper bound

    V<=E^2 C(T,A,M)/sigma + the quantity in (23).        (24)

For eta<=0 the same bound follows from (15) by dropping its nonpositive
residual term; thus (24) covers that case as well.

The original ruler has A/T<=rho=c_m-2 and E/N<=rho<1/2.
Appendix C sharpens (22) to retain A/T before taking the original-pair
supremum. This additional dependence is essential when a large part of
the word lies before the first checkpoint. Formula (24) alone is not
asserted to give a uniform whole-pair comparison.

## 6. Whole parameter comparison for one structural class of ORIGINAL pairs

Fix original enclosing m>=128, put lambda=1+sqrt2, and use beta_m=1-1/m,
c_m=(1+sqrt2)(1+1/m), a_m=beta_m/c_m, and original recursively nested
words H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m c_m^j,H_j). Every child keeps these
SAME enclosing-m parameters. Write M_j=1+sum H_j and rho=c_m-2.

Consider ALL pairs whose first selected checkpoint lies inside the
particular H_l immediately preceding their second level-l checkpoint.
This entails l>=1. Let x be its local prefix mass plus1 in that H_l.
The original middle mass M and first excess A satisfy

    x+M+A=M_l=:V_l, T>=x, N>=V_l,
    A<=rho min(x,M), E<=rho V_l.                       (25)

The side bounds follow from the original adjacent H_k copies around
the first checkpoint, with a_k<=rho M_k as proved in Appendix B.
The full original mass is S=T+A+M+E+N. For either summand in (18),
its product with(2S-1) decreases as T or N increases while the other
listed quantities are fixed. For N this is the decreasing ratio
(2S-1)/(2N-1). For T each term is a positive constant times
(2T+C)/(T+D)^2 with C>2D, whose derivative is negative for T>=0.
For the first term D=0; for the second D=A+M and
C=2(A+M+E+N)-1>2D. For the pair term D=0 also.

We may therefore evaluate the upper bound at T=x,N=V_l. There
S=2V_l+E=M_(l+1). Put r_0=A/x and z_0=M/x. By (25),
r_0<=rho min(1,z_0), E/M<=rho[1+(1+r_0)/z_0]. If z_0>=1,
r_0<=rho and E/M<=rho(2+rho). If z_0<=1, then
r_0<=rho z_0 and
r_0 E/M<=rho^2[1+(1+rho)z_0]<=rho^2(2+rho).
In both cases

    (A/x)^2+(AE/(xM))^2<=rho^2+[c_m rho^2]^2.

The other branch in (18) has
(A/x)^2+(E/V_l)^2<=2rho^2<=rho^2+[c_m rho^2]^2,
because c_m rho>1: c_m>1+sqrt2 and that root obeys c_m(c_m-2)=1
at equality. Thus ALL parameters in this structural pair class obey

    (2S-1)V <= [rho^2+(c_m rho^2)^2]
               (2M_(l+1)-1)/(2M_l-1).                (26)

For m>=128, Appendix B gives rho<=9/20,c_m<=49/20,c_m rho^2<1/2.
Also a_m>2/5: using sqrt2<10/7 gives
a_m> (127/129)(7/17)=889/2193>2/5. Hence M_l>=M_1=2+a_m>12/5.
As M_(l+1)<=c_m M_l,

    (2M_(l+1)-1)/(2M_l-1)
       <=c_m+(c_m-1)/(2M_l-1)<=269/95.

Consequently the ENTIRE obtuse family over ALL pairs described by (25)
satisfies

    V<=(48689/38000) K_n(H_m), m>=128,                 (27)
    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) sup V
       <=2(48689/38000)/exp(1)<1.

Here48689/38000<4/3 and exp(1)>8/3 (its first five power-series terms
sum to65/24). Thus (27) is already a genuine original-target obstruction
for this whole pair class, even though its finite factor exceeds1.
It is not a claim that a tuple attaining that factor exists.

For a strict SAME-word reference comparison, take m>=1024. The rational
bound sqrt2<99/70 gives
c_m<(169/70)(1025/1024)<29/12, so rho<5/12 and
c_m rho^2<725/1728<3/7. The preceding mass ratio is at most53/19.
Equation (26) therefore gives

    V<=gamma_nested K_n(H_m)=gamma_nested F_ray_n(H_m),
    gamma_nested=(25/144+9/49)(53/19)
                 =133613/134064<1.                    (28)

The full Huber-augmented lens reference also dominates this SAME K value;
Appendix B retains its distinction from the full word-dependent complex
legal set. The maximum of the Huber value and the FULL word-dependent
complex channel also dominates the lens maximum, since Lens_n is a
subset of D_h by accepted dfde2fa326c908bf. No inequality against the
unaugmented complex component alone is claimed. No child word has been
assigned different parameters.

This closes EVERY obtuse parameter tuple on the nested-before-second
pair class. Appendix C treats every pair whose first checkpoint is
outside that particular preceding H_l, covering both the reverse-nested
case and a higher checkpoint strictly between the selected indices.

## 7. A new legal positive-residual state, not a benchmark gain

On any physical two-checkpoint word choose k=E/(2N) and

    theta=min{A/4, A^2/[32N(1+k)^2],
                   A^2 E/[32NM(1+k)]}>0,
    u=kappa=0, delta=2theta, alpha=A-2theta,
    v=sqrt(theta/M), beta=Mv,
    ell=k v, w=(1+k)theta/alpha.

Then alpha>=A/2. Prefix activity holds with vH=2theta=delta.
At the middle end, R_q=theta and eta=theta, so G=0, while
a_q-c=(1+k)v^2>0. The terminal q margin is

    L_q=(1+k)(E-Nk)v^2-Nw^2
       =(1+k)E theta/(2M)-Nw^2>0,

since Nw^2<=4N(1+k)^2theta^2/A^2 is at most
(1+k)E theta/(8M). The same estimate also gives Nw^2<=theta/8.
Thus L_0=theta+k(E-Nk)v^2-Nw^2>=7theta/8>0.
Moreover Nw/alpha<=1/[8(1+k)]<1, so the final proximal first
coordinate -alpha+Nw is negative; its p-plane is negative and (6)
holds. All tail q margins hold because a_r-c>0 and its final one
is positive. These explicit checks invoke (9) for every directed row.
The minimizer is uniquely0 and

    R^2=(T+2theta)^2+4Mtheta,
    P=2theta+theta E^2(2N+1)/(4MN^2)
                 -sigma(1+k)^2theta^2/(A-2theta)^2.    (29)

Both the middle reserve and positive contribution eta are theta;
neither is deleted. Every exact holding cost remains (11).

For the actual first and last level-0 checkpoints of ORIGINAL H_m,
T=N=1,A=E=a_m<1/2 and M=M_m-2-2A. The minimum defining theta is
its third entry: theta=A^3/[32M(1+A/2)]. This follows from M>=1,
A<1/2 by comparing that expression separately with the other two.
By (29), P<=(35/16)theta and R^2>=1. Since
2M_m-1=2M+3+4A<=2M+5<=7M,

    V/K_n(H_m)<=(245/512) A^3/(1+A/2)<245/4096<1.     (30)

This is a genuine positive-residual static law in the
complement of condition (17), eta>0,E^2<2M sigma, with every physical query paid. Its
complete SAME-word pricing is weak. It is not an improving adversary.

## 8. Counterexample screening and the original-target gap

The old sign-deletion shortcut is not reused: (15) retains2eta.
The new state in Section7 has eta>0 and positive middle reserve, so
the restricted eta<=0 case cannot be silently treated as exhaustive.
There is no counterexample claimed against the proved inequalities
(12),(19),(22),(26); these have direct proofs above, not a search verdict.

A tempting relaxation using the middle row alone allows positive eta
without the transport cost (19). Testing u=0, a middle q/r tie,
v^2=eta/M, beta=Mv, ell=k v and w=(1+k)eta/alpha exposes its failure:
the actual terminal q row demands

    N(1+k)^2 eta^2/alpha^2
       <=(1+k)(E-Nk)eta/M.

In particular k=1 is impossible when E<N. This diagnoses an ILLEGAL
relaxation; it is not an actual-word counterexample to any legal-family
upper bound. Section7 instead enforces this exact row and prices the
result against complete references. No numerical counterexample search
or machine algebra has been performed.

Appendix C completes the quantitative comparison in the domain
eta>0,E^2<2M(2N-1), including every first checkpoint outside the H_l
preceding the second. It gives an explicit finite upper bound and a
uniform analytical asymptotic price, rather than leaving a feasibility
profile as an unexplained technical lemma.

The original target remains OPEN. This obstruction depends on exactly
three gradient-holding blocks, p=(1,0), q=(-u,-v) with u>=0,v>0,
r=(-w,ell) with w,ell>0, independent nonnegative alpha,beta, and affine
costs0,kappa,0 with kappa>=0. A next mechanism must change at least one
of these hypotheses: for example first/final relative affine costs,
the terminal half-plane, the middle sign pattern, or the number of
gradient changes/variation within ordinary physical blocks. C99/C100
are not imported as unrestricted exclusions. No full-class upper
certificate or uniform arbitrary-word lower bound is produced here.

Additional symbolic boundary attacks on the complete comparison were
performed after Appendix C was assembled. At r_0^2=e_0/(1+2e_0), both
branches of C_res equal r_0^2 and D_C=1; no branch jump occurs. At
r_0=e_0=1/2, C_res=1/4, and with M=N=1 the exact residual bound is
3/16, agreeing with (C7). In the limit e_0 decreasing to0 with r_0 fixed,
C_res tends to r_0^2/2, so the same-side residual case is retained.
The D=0 radius branch can occur only in the eta<=u alpha part of the
reserve proof, since eta>u alpha would make its displayed vertical
radius lower bound strictly positive. For Q bounded and T unbounded,
the direct bound (18) tends to0 after original normalization; using only
the coarser C9 after reducing the prefix would miss this limiting case.
For Q unbounded, both N/M endpoints and all finite positive limits are
covered in C.4. These are author consistency checks with no independent
verification; no legal counterexample to the stated bounds was found.
